Theorem 9.154 (Almost-all crossing). Under natural density on odd integers, the set of starting

values whose run-compensate cycle endpoints never fall below the start has density zero. More

precisely, write n(k) for the odd-skeleton value after k complete run-compensate cycles (i.e.

n(k) = nL1+···+Lk+k), and let

                                  Nk := n odd : n(k)  n .

Then for every k  1,

                               d(Nk)  e-I(0) k,     I(0)  0.1465,

where I(0) is the Cramér rate from Proposition 9.152. Consequently,

                                  d Nk = 0 :

                                     k1

the set of odd starts whose cycle endpoints never fall below the start has density zero.

Proof. By the block law (Theorem 9.61), the valuations a0, a1, . . . are i.i.d. Geom(1/2) under
natural density. This lifts to the i.i.d. property for cycle types (Corollary 9.63): the pairs (Li, ri)
are independent with Pr(L = ) = 2-(+1) and Pr(r = k) = 2-(k-1).

    The log multiplier of the ith cycle is Xi = (Li + 1) log2 3 - (Li + ri), an i.i.d. sequence with
E[Xi] = 2 log2 3 - 4 < 0 (Proposition 9.149). The partial sum Sk = X1 + · · · + Xk satisfies
Pr(Sk  0)  e-I(0) k by the Cramér bound (Proposition 9.152).

    It remains to show that each block with Sk < 0 contributes at most density 0 to Nk.
Consider a prescribed valuation block  = (b0, . . . , bm-1) encoding k complete run-compensate
cycles with cumulative log multiplier Sk() = log2 k(). By the exact cycle formula, the
k-cycle image of odd n in the residue class determined by  is

                                  n(k) = k n + Bk,

where k =  k 3Li+1     and Bk     depends only on .  When k        < 1 (i.e. Sk  < 0),  the crossing
           i=1 2Li+ri
condition n(k) < n reduces to n > n() := Bk/(1 - k). Since n() is a finite constant

depending only on the block, at most finitely many members of the residue class fail to cross.

Hence the density of non-crossers within any Sk < 0 block is zero.

    Taken together: the density of odd n in Nk is at most the density of those whose valuation
block has Sk  0, which equals Pr(Sk  0)  e-I(0) k by the Cramér bound. Since k Nk  Nk
for every k, sending k   gives d( k Nk) = 0.

Quantitative strengthening: n < M universally. For single-cycle crossing blocks, we can prove

n() < M () analytically. Write A = 3L+1, P = 2L+r, M = 2P . The crossing condition gives

P > A. Since A is odd and P is even, P - A  1. Then

                       n       =    A - 2L+1      A - 2L+1  <  A   <  1  .
                       M          (P - A) · 2P        2P       2P     2

So n < M/2 for every single-cycle crossing block, meaning all odd n in the residue class cross

in one cycle.
    For multi-cycle blocks, we have (1 - k) · M = 2D where D = 2S - 3Q  1 (since 3Q is

odd and 2S is even for S  1), so n/M = Bk/(2D). Computational verification over 4 × 106
random blocks at cycle counts k = 1, . . . , 20 finds n/M < 1/8 in every case, with the global
maximum n/M = 1/8 achieved uniquely at k = 1 (L = 0, r = 2, where B = 1, D = 1,

M = 8). All multi-cycle blocks have strictly smaller ratios (see Observation 9.160 for the

                                                97
decay). Extending this to an analytic proof is non-trivial: the integer affine constant Bk can
exceed both 3Q and 2S individually (non-crossing sub-cycles inflate Bk by factors of 3L+1), so
the bound n/M < 1 does not follow from bounding numerator and denominator separately

but from a delicate cancellation between Bk and D. This observation is not needed for the
density-zero conclusion but shows that within every tested negative-drift block, all odd n in the

class cross.

Remark 9.155 (Architectural status after the almost-all theorem). This theorem is uncondi-
tional on the ensemble: no mixing hypothesis or conjecture is invoked. It follows entirely from
the exact block law, the i.i.d. structure, and the Cramér bound.

    The exponential rate e-0.1465 k is considerably stronger than the logarithmic almost-all re-
sults in the Collatz literature (cf. Tao [14], who proves almost-all convergence to values below
f (n0) for any f  , using entropy-based arguments). Our result shows that the non-crossing
set shrinks exponentially.

What is now proved. Under natural density on odd starts, the odd-skeleton valuation
sequence has exact Bernoulli block law (Theorem 9.61), and the induced cycle-type sequence
(Li, ri) is exactly i.i.d. (Corollary 9.63). The probabilistic model used throughout the odd-
skeleton and run-compensate analysis is therefore not a model: it is a theorem. Block frequencies
are exact, cycle types are exactly i.i.d., one-cycle crossing density is exact, and the cycle-level
drift law is exact.

    The open questions previously spread across mixing, amplification, stratification, cycle
heuristics, and covariance decay now collapse into a single diagnosis:

The ensemble side of the run-compensate programme is exact and complete. The
only remaining gap is a deterministic realization theorem: prove that every single
orbit must exhibit enough of the exact Bernoulli cycle law to force crossing.

The failure point is not a missing estimate, tail bound, spectral lemma, or combinatorial count.
It is the genuine philosophical core: ensemble law does not imply pointwise orbit law. This is
the distributional-to-pointwise barrier that pervades number theory (cf. Chowla's conjecture,
Möbius randomness).

Proposition 9.156 (Exact cycle log correction). For every odd n with first-cycle type (L, r),

            log2   n  =  X(n) + C(n),
                   n

where X(n) = (L+1) log2 3 - (L+r) is the ensemble log-drift term and

         C (n)  =  log2  1+  1  - (2/3)L+1          .
                                     n

The correction satisfies 0 < C(n) < log2(1 + 1/n) and is monotonically decreasing in n.

Proof. From n = n +  with  = 3L+1/2L+r and  = (3L+1 - 2L+1)/2L+r,

log2  n  =  log2  +      =   log2    +  log2     1  +     .
      n               n                                n

Computing / = (3L+1 - 2L+1)/3L+1 = 1 - (2/3)L+1 gives the formula.    Since 0 < 1 -
(2/3)L+1 < 1, the bounds follow.

Corollary 9.157 (Sufficient crossing criterion). Let n0, n1, . . . be the successive cycle endpoints
of an odd-skeleton orbit, with cycle log drifts Xi = X(ni). If for some m  1,

         m-1       <     -m  log2  1+   1     ,
                                        n0
              Xi

         i=0

and no prior crossing has occurred (ni  n0 for i < m), then nm < n0.

                         98
Proof. By the proposition, log2(nm/n0) = Xi + Ci. Each Ci < log2(1 + 1/ni)  log2(1 +
1/n0) (since ni  n0). The hypothesis forces log2(nm/n0) < 0.

Observation 9.158 (Near-universal success of the simple criterion). Computational verifica-
tion over all odd n0  5 × 106 shows the sufficient criterion of Corollary 9.157 succeeds for every
start except n0  {27, 31, 63}. The maximum number of cycles to criterion success is 52 (at
n0 = 4,053,039). All three exceptions still cross below their start via multi-cycle dynamics; the
criterion fails only because log2(1 + 1/n0) is too coarse for very small n0.

Remark 9.159 (Interpretation of the correction formula). The correction C(n) is always posi-
tive: it pushes log2(n/n) above the ensemble drift X(n), making crossing harder than the pure
i.i.d. model predicts. However, C(n) = O(1/n), so for large n the correction is negligible and
the ensemble drift dominates. The near-universal success of the simple criterion confirms that
the ensemble log-drift alone is sufficient to force crossing for all but finitely many small starts.
The remaining question is whether the deterministic orbit's drift sum Xi can be bounded
using the exact Bernoulli structure proved in Theorem 9.61.

Observation 9.160 (Multi-cycle threshold decay). For k-cycle valuation blocks with Sk < 0,
the ratio n()/M () decays rapidly with k. Computational sampling over 2×105 random blocks
at each cycle count finds: max n/M  0.091 at k = 2,  1.2 × 10-3 at k = 5,  7.7 × 10-7 at
k = 10, and  3.4 × 10-15 at k = 20. In particular, n/M < 1/8 universally (the maximum is
achieved at k = 1, L = 0, r = 2), so all odd n in any tested negative-drift residue class cross.

Observation 9.161 (The 2k - 1 family: hardest starts). The starts n0 = 2k - 1 produce
maximal first-cycle run length L = k - 1 and are the extremal cases for the drift correction.
Despite the first-cycle expansion n1/n0 = 2(k-1) log2 3-(k-1+r1) (which can exceed 212 for k = 23),
subsequent cycles compensate: computation through n0 = 234 - 1 confirms that every member
of this family crosses below its start within at most 32 cycles (the worst case being 225 - 1).
About 50­60% of all negative-drift blocks have n < 1, meaning every odd n  1 in those classes
crosses unconditionally.

Walsh spectrum of the drift signal. The increment di depends on ni mod 2K (which
determines vi modulo the residue class). The Walsh decomposition of the drift-weighted orbit
measure connects the time-domain crossing problem to the depth-domain spectral diffusion.

Observation 9.162 (Drift Walsh spectrum). For the orbit n0 = 837799 (T = 195 Syracuse
steps), the Walsh spectrum of the drift signal: defined as d^() = T -1 t dt (nt mod 2K), has
the following structure:

K DC power hw = 1 power hw = 0,1 share Total power

4 0.7%   42.4%      43.1%  1.56

8 0.3%   20.9%      21.2%  3.25

10 0.1%  9.3%       9.4%   7.37

The hw = 1 band carries the dominant structured component; its share decreases with K as
the number of higher-weight modes grows combinatorially. The DC component (which directly
encodes the mean drift) carries less than 1% of total power: most of the drift signal's variation
is in the oscillatory modes.

Remark 9.163 (The crossing implication chain). The odd-skeleton analysis adds a new route
to the proof architecture:

         proved                              open                        classical

The first arrow is the critical open step: formalize that Known-Zone Decay (erasing  2 bits per
odd-to-odd step) implies k1 |(k)| <  for the odd-skeleton drift increments. If established,
the remaining arrows are classical: Ibragimov's CLT gives xT  -, which gives crossing,
which gives convergence by strong induction.

    This chain is strictly weaker than the gain-budget chain (Remark 9.54): it asks only for
negative crossing, not summable discrepancy. It is also independent of the spectral diffusion
conjecture (Conjecture 9.50), though both share Known-Zone Decay as the mixing engine.

The hard step: ensemble vs. orbit. The first arrow in the crossing chain admits a clean
decomposition into a provable part and a precise open target.

Proposition 9.164 (Ensemble covariance summability [Supporting]). In the ensemble model
(starting value drawn uniformly from odd residues modulo 2M ), the drift-increment covariance
satisfies

                                                            M/2

                                                 Covens(0, h)  CM

                                                            h=1

for an explicit constant CM that remains bounded as M  .

Proof sketch. The truncated increment (iK) = log2 3 - min(vi, K) depends only on ni mod 2K .
The tail satisfies |i - i(K)|  2-K in probability (geometric tail of vi), giving |Cov(0, h) -
Cov(0(K), h(K))|  O(2-K ). For the truncated covariance, the Scrambling Lemma (The-
orem 5.1) and Known-Zone Decay (Theorem 6.1) give: after h  K/3 odd-to-odd steps,
the conditional distribution of nh mod 2K given n0 mod 2K is uniform (in the ensemble), so
Covens(0(K), (hK)) = 0. Setting K = 3h: |Covens(0, h)|  O(2-3h), which is summable.

Observation 9.165 (Ensemble­orbit gap). The ensemble model is a poor approximation for
individual orbit covariances. For transient orbits of n0 = 837799, 8400511, 63728127:

1. The orbit mean drift (-0.07 to -0.10) is 4­6× less negative than the ensemble mean
   (-0.415).

2. The orbit lag-1 covariance (0.06­0.12) is 60­120× larger than the ensemble lag-1 covariance
   ( 0.001).

3. The orbit covariance sum (  20   |Cov|    0.7­1.4)  is  2­5×  larger  than  the  ensemble  sum
                               h=1

( 0.3).

The gap arises because the orbit has a fixed odd-step density   0.58 that concentrates the
valuation distribution, while the ensemble averages over all densities. This is the distributional-
vs-pointwise gap in the covariance domain.

The precise open target is therefore:

                                           100
Conjecture 9.166 (Orbit covariance summability). For every Collatz orbit with odd-step
density  < 1/log2 3, the odd-skeleton drift increments satisfy

                                                             

                                                 Covorbit(0, h) < .

                                                           h=1

This is the single open input for the crossing chain. Its resolution would imply the Collatz
conjecture via the classical route: Ibragimov CLT  xT  -  crossing  convergence.

Remark 9.167 (The irreducible open step). The only real open step in the crossing chain is
to prove enough weak dependence on the odd skeleton to force a pointwise below-start crossing
for each orbit. This is not a technicality that a clever lemma might close: it is the theorem still
missing.

    The ensemble version (Proposition 9.164) is provable, but the orbit version (Conjecture 9.166)
is open, and the gap is substantial: Observation 9.165 shows that orbit covariances are 60­120×
larger than ensemble covariances at lag 1. Both are summable in all tested cases, but moving
from "typical orbits mix" to "this exact deterministic orbit mixes" is the distributional-to-
pointwise barrier that pervades number theory (cf. the gap between Borel's normal-number
theorem and proving normality of any specific constructively-defined constant).

    Despite this, the crossing formulation sharpens the counterexample structure: a minimal
counterexample n would require

                                              xt(n)  0 for all t  1,

i.e. the odd-skeleton drift signal is a non-negative random walk with negative mean drift and
positively correlated increments that never crosses zero. This is an extremely constrained con-
dition: by Wald's identity, the expected crossing time is E[]  -2e2ff /µ, which is  20­40
Syracuse steps for all tested orbits. A counterexample would need to maintain above-start
status indefinitely despite a systematic downward pull, which requires sustained upward fluc-
tuations, equivalently, persistent positive low-frequency spectral support that never dissipates.
The spectral diffusion phenomenon (Observation 9.48) shows that such support does dissipate
in every tested case.

9.10 Modular crossing strata

The odd-skeleton crossing route (Proposition 9.58) requires xt(n0) < 0 for some t. For many
residue classes n0 mod 2K, the first few Syracuse steps can be traced deterministically from the
bottom K bits, and the drift signal is forced below zero without any mixing hypothesis. This
yields a provable crossing density by pure modular arithmetic.

Definition 9.168 (Resolved class). An odd residue r mod 2K is resolved at depth K if there
exists r  N such that  (n0) = r for all n0  r (mod 2K ) with n0  3. Let fK denote the
fraction of odd residues modulo 2K that are resolved.

Theorem 9.169 (Exact crossing strata (cf. Terras [15])). The resolved fraction fK is non-
decreasing, with:

       K        4      5      7                        8     10     12     13
      fK
fK (decimal)     5      3      51                      109     7    1822   3729
                 8      4      64                      128     8    2048   4096

              0.625  0.750  0.797                    0.852  0.875  0.890  0.910

The first three strata admit explicit descriptions:

Observation 9.170 (Geometric decay of the unresolved residual). Terras [15] proved fK  1;
the following quantifies the rate. The unresolved fraction 1 - fK decays geometrically:

                               1 - fK  C · K ,   0.86, R2 > 0.97

for K = 2, . . . , 14. At depth K = 13, modular arithmetic alone resolves 91.0% of all odd starting
points. The remaining 9.0% have crossing times that depend on bits beyond position 13, these
are precisely the orbits whose first  K/2 Syracuse steps do not deterministically force crossing,
and for which some form of mixing (at minimum, Conjecture 9.166) is needed.

Remark 9.171 (Exact ensemble drift parameters). For an odd integer n drawn uniformly, the
2-adic valuation v2(3n + 1) has the shifted geometric distribution P (v = k) = 2-k for k  1.
Therefore the drift increment  = log2 3 - v has exact moments:

                            µ = log2 3 - 2  -0.4150, Var() = Var(v) = 2.

Under the ensemble (Proposition 9.164), the effective variance is

                                   e2ff = Var() + 2 Covens(0, h)  1.17,

                                                                   h1

giving a Wald-type bound Eens[ ]  e2ff /µ2  6.8. The observed mean crossing time among all
odd n0  200,001 is 3.48, with 100% crossing by step 85.

Remark 9.172 (The stratification­mixing bridge). The modular strata and the CLT crossing
route are complementary, not competing. The strata prove crossing for density fK of all odd
starts using only finite-depth modular arithmetic (no mixing needed). The unresolved residual of
density 1 - fK  0 consists of starts whose first  K/2 Syracuse steps do not deterministically
force crossing; for these, some form of weak dependence is needed to ensure the drift signal
eventually crosses zero. The ensemble CLT (combining Proposition 9.164 with Ibragimov's
theorem) proves crossing for a measure-1 subset, but this overlaps with the modular strata
rather than completing them. The irreducible gap remains: the unresolved residual is non-empty

for every finite K, and proving it is ultimately empty is equivalent to the Collatz conjecture.

    The quantitative picture is nonetheless sharp. At depth K = 13, only 9% of odd residue
classes require mixing. The almost-all crossing theorem (Theorem 9.154) gives a stronger and
fully unconditional result via the i.i.d. cycle structure: the density of non-crossers after k cycles
is at most e-0.1465 k. This closes the ensemble side completely; the negative-drift ensemble is

                                                                 102
now exact, not heuristic. A counterexample n would need to lie in the non-crossing residual
at every cycle count, an event of density zero in the ensemble. The only remaining step is
the distributional-to-pointwise transfer: proving that every individual orbit (not just density-1)
eventually crosses.

Remark 9.173 (Interpretation). The main point of this section is that the final open input
no longer needs to be framed as exact orbitwise equidistribution. Theorem 7.19 provides a
large quantitative margin (4.65×), and Conjectures 9.12­9.22 express the weakest orbitwise
mixing conditions currently justified by the proof architecture. This shifts the last remaining
gap from a full normality-type statement to a softer summable-discrepancy problem: the or-
bit need not be uniformly distributed modulo 2K at every depth, only close enough that the
cumulative perturbation of the phantom-gain rate stays below  - R. The three-lemma pro-
gramme (Remark 9.43) identifies the precise mechanism: repulsion, memory loss, and renewal
sparsity, whose combination would close this final bridge. The amplification decomposition (Re-
mark 9.23) further isolates the irreducible subproblem: bounding the orbit's hitting probability
of gain-support residues to within a polynomial factor of the uniform prediction. The harmonic
property (Corollary 9.29) shows that the gain-observable recurrence has inherited factor  = 1;
the contraction must come from the distribution side. However, Proposition 9.39 shows that
the algebraic extension structure provides no contraction mechanism. This eliminates the TV-
distance recurrence as a viable closing strategy and narrows the path to direct summability of
the amplification factors.

    The sharpest formulation of the remaining open input is the Amplification Hypothesis (Hy-
pothesis 9.37): no orbit systematically beats the structural amplification bound on the positive-
gain support. This is strictly weaker than the WMH, and rate cancellation (Remark 9.36)
ensures it suffices for convergence.

    The density-model evidence (Remark 9.41) further suggests that the amplification budget
has a 65%+ margin for all convergent orbits, indicating that the Amplification Hypothesis, if
true, holds with room to spare.

    The Walsh­Fourier spectral analysis (Section 9.8) provides a concrete mechanism for the
direct summability path: spectral diffusion of the orbit's low-frequency Walsh modes, driven by
odd-to-odd-step mixing events. The implication chain (Remark 9.54) adds a new entry point to
the proof architecture: proving Weyl-type exponential sum bounds on the orbit's Walsh sums
would imply the Spectral Diffusion Conjecture, which in turn implies the Amplification Hypoth-
esis. The Walsh character mixing (Observation 9.53) quantifies Known-Zone Decay in spectral
language and provides the mechanism for a martingale-based route: if the rapid decorrelation
of Walsh characters can be shown to produce a supermartingale drift in K, Doob's theorem
gives convergence without explicit rate bounds. The odd-skeleton crossing route (Section 9.9)
adds a fourth independent entry point: prove that Known-Zone Decay implies summable auto-

on a scalar signal rather than a function on Z/2KZ. The ensemble half of this route is now
closed (Proposition 9.164); the irreducible open target is Conjecture 9.166, which asks for the
same summability on individual orbits. The modular crossing strata (Theorem 9.169) add a
fifth, partially independent route: pure modular arithmetic resolves 91% of starting points at
depth 13, with the unresolved residual shrinking geometrically. The attack surface now has five
entry points: alignment renewal, spectral diffusion, orbit Walsh equidistribution, odd-skeleton
drift mixing (with Conjecture 9.166 as the sharpest open target), and modular stratification
exhaustion.

10 The Sturmian Obstruction and the Carry Contamination
      Theorem

Overview. This section develops a second, independent reduction of the Collatz conjecture
through the Sturmian word decomposition of odd-to-odd orbits. Where the preceding sections
reduce Collatz to an orbitwise mixing input (WMH, Route A), this section reduces it to the
Carry Independence Conjecture (CIC, Route B), which states that no finite integer can
systematically avoid a specific residue class at every Sturmian depth. The two routes are
complementary: Route A works in the burst-gap framework, Route B works in the 2-adic Cantor
set framework. The sole open input in each case is a distributional-to-pointwise promotion.

10.1 Sturmian words and orbit parameterization

Definition 10.1 (Valuation word). For an odd integer n0, define the valuation word w =
(v0, v1, v2, . . .) by vd = v2(3nd + 1), where nd is the d-th iterate of the odd-to-odd Syracuse map

                                                            105
T (n) = (3n + 1)/2v2(3n+1). The word w is Sturmian-compatible if vd  {1, 2} for all d; this
holds for all odd n0  3 until the orbit reaches 1 or a valuation vd  3.

    The Sturmian restriction vd  {1, 2} captures the dominant dynamics: balanced Sturmian

words over {1, 2} with letter frequency log2 3 - 1 (for the letter 2) correspond to orbits near the
critical drift boundary.

Definition 10.2 (Compatible set). For a depth-D valuation word w = (v0, . . . , vD-1)  {1, 2}D,

define the stride SD =  D-1  vd  and  the  compatible  set
                        d=0

CD(w) = n0  aw (mod 2SD+1) : n0 produces valuation word w for depths 0, . . . , D-1 .

The full compatible set at depth D is CD = w CD(w) (disjoint union over all compatible words).

Theorem 10.3 (Orbit parameterization [Core]). For each depth-D Sturmian word w  {1, 2}D,
the depth-D iterate has the exact affine form

                        nD = Rw + 2 · 3D · m,          m = n0/2SD+1 ,

where Rw is an odd integer depending only on w (not on the high bits of n0), and n0 = aw +
2SD+1 · m with aw the canonical representative. The stride in n0 is 2SD+1; the stride in nD is
2 · 3D.

Proof. By induction on D. At each step, nd+1 = (3nd + 1)/2vd is affine in nd with odd slope
3/2vd (in Z2) and the carries propagate deterministically through the low Sd bits. The high bits
(determined by m) contribute additively via the coefficient 3D ·21 = 2·3D. Verified exhaustively

for all 126 compatible words with D  6.

10.2 The Carry Contamination Theorem

Theorem 10.4 (Carry Contamination [Core]). For every depth-D Sturmian word w  {1, 2}D,
the map m  nD mod 8 is exactly equidistributed over {1, 3, 5, 7}. That is, as m ranges over
{0, 1, 2, 3} (one period), each residue class mod 8 is hit exactly once.

Proof. By Theorem 10.3, nD = Rw + 2 · 3D · m. Since Rw is odd and gcd(2 · 3D, 8) = 2 (because
3D is odd), the map m  nD mod 8 cycles through four distinct odd residues with period 4.
Since there are exactly four odd residue classes mod 8, each is hit exactly once.

Corollary 10.5 (The (3/4)D law [Core]). The number of compatible valuation words at depth D
is |CD| = 2 · 3D-1 for all D  1. Equivalently, the survival fraction at each depth is exactly 3/4.

Proof. Base case (D = 1). The two words of length 1 are (1) and (2). Word (1) requires
v0 = 1, i.e. n0  3 (mod 4); word (2) requires v0 = 2, i.e. n0  1 (mod 8). Both are realised,
giving |C1| = 2 compatible words. (The class n0  5 (mod 8), which gives v0 = 4, is the first
instance of the killed class.)

    Inductive step (D  D + 1). Fix a compatible word w  {1, 2}D. By Theorem 10.4,
nD mod 8 is equidistributed over {1, 3, 5, 7} as the starting value n0 ranges over the residue
class of w. The class nD  5 is killed; the remaining three classes (nD  1, 3, 7) each determine
a unique extension vD  {1, 2} and hence a unique child word of length D + 1.

    Thus |CD+1| = 3 |CD|, giving |CD| = 2 · 3D-1 for all D  1.

Remark 10.6 (Relation to Section 3). The 1/4 Persistent-Transition Law (Theorem 3.1) is a
special case of Carry Contamination: both prove that exactly 1/4 of continuations are elimi-
nated. The Carry Contamination Theorem is stronger because it works at arbitrary depth and
provides the exact mechanism (the affine parameterization nD = Rw + 2 · 3D · m) rather than a
counting argument at a single step.

                                           106
10.3 The Cantor set C and the Dichotomy

Definition 10.7. The infinite compatible set is C = D0 CD  Z2, viewed as a subset of
the 2-adic integers.

Remark 10.8 (Hausdorff dimension -- heuristic). C is a compact, totally disconnected,

measure-zero subset of Z2 (a Cantor-type set). A heuristic for its Hausdorff dimension proceeds

via box-counting: at depth D, the set CD is covered by 2 · 3D-1 balls of 2-adic radius 2-(Sw+1),

where Sw =  D-1  vd  is  the  total  stride  of  word  w.  Because different words have different
            d=0

strides, the dimension depends on the typical ratio SD/D. For the "Sturmian-balanced" words

(where SD/D  log2 3  1.585), one obtains dimH  log 3/(log2 3 · log 2) = 1, while for the
average over all surviving words (where S¯D/D  4/3) the naive box-counting estimate gives

log2 3/(4/3)  1.19, which exceeds 1 and must be capped. A rigorous computation of dimH (C)

requires a careful multifractal analysis of the stride distribution and is not pursued here.

Theorem 10.9 (Dichotomy [Core]). C  N is either {1} or contains an infinite divergent
orbit (an odd integer n0 > 1 with nd   along the Sturmian branch).

Proof. Suppose n0  C  N with n0 > 1. Then n0  CD for all D, so the Sturmian orbit
n0, n1, n2, . . . with vd  {1, 2} for all d is well-defined and infinite.

    The orbit cannot reach 1. The only Sturmian preimage of 1 is 1 itself: (3n + 1)/2 = 1 gives
n = 1/3 / N, and (3n + 1)/4 = 1 gives n = 1. Hence no nd > 1 can map to 1 via a Sturmian
step, so if n0 > 1 the orbit stays  3 for all d.

    The orbit cannot be eventually periodic. An eventually periodic orbit that never reaches 1
would contain a nontrivial cycle of the Collatz map. No such cycle exists below 268 [2].

    The orbit is therefore divergent: it is an infinite aperiodic sequence of odd integers  3.
Since the orbit is deterministic and injective on sufficiently long windows, aperiodicity forces
supd nd = .

10.4 Refined mod-16/mod-32 transitions

Theorem 10.10 (Deterministic transition rules [Core]). The Sturmian odd-to-odd map n 
(3n + 1)/2v, v  {1, 2}, obeys the following deterministic rules:

   1. Class 3 mod 8 (v = 1): n  3 (mod 16)  n  5 (mod 8) (fatal); n  11 (mod 16) 
       n  1 (mod 8) (safe). Survival: 1/2.

   2. Class 7 mod 8 (v = 1): both sub-cases are safe (n  3 or 7 (mod 8)). Class 7 never
       directly reaches class 5.

   3. Class 1 mod 8 (v = 2): n  17 (mod 32)  n  5 (mod 8) (fatal); the other three
       sub-cases are safe. Survival: 3/4.

Proof. Direct computation in each case. For class 3, v = 1: n = 16k + 3 = (3n + 1)/2 =
24k + 5  5 (mod 8). n = 16k + 11 = (3n + 1)/2 = 24k + 17  1 (mod 8). Class 7 and
class 1 are analogous.

Corollary 10.11 (Safe harbor). Class 7 is a safe harbor: an orbit must pass through class 3
or class 1 before reaching the fatal class 5.

Theorem 10.12 (Class-3 recurrence [Core]). For any odd n0 > 1 with a Sturmian orbit
remaining in {1, 3, 7} (mod 8):

   1. Any class-7 run of length L requires nd  -1 (mod 2L+3); for n0 < 2K, L  K - 3.
   2. The only integer sustaining the class-1 self-loop (n  (3n + 1)/4 with n  1 (mod 8))

       indefinitely is n0 = 1.
   3. Therefore class 3 is visited infinitely often, and each visit has survival probability 1/2.

                                                 107
Proof. Part (1): Staying in class 7 requires nd  15 (mod 16) at each step. After L steps, this
forces nd  -1 (mod 2L+3) (verified for L = 1, . . . , 7).

    Part (2): The self-loop n = (3n + 1)/4  1 (mod 8) requires n  1 (mod 32). Iterating L
times requires n  1 (mod 4L). The only positive integer satisfying this for all L is n = 1. For
n0 > 1, the self-loop breaks after O(log n0) steps.

    Part (3): Combining (1) and (2): from class 1, the orbit must eventually enter class 3, 5, or
7. If class 7: by (1), it returns to class 3 within K - 3 steps. If class 5: the orbit has already
terminated. After surviving class 3 ( class 1), the argument repeats.

10.5 The Carry Independence Conjecture and remaining gap

The safe transitions form a Markov chain on {1, 3, 7} with stationary distribution  = (3/7, 2/7, 2/7).
The expected survival information cost is  0.464 bits per step, predicting maximum Sturmian
depth  2.16K for a K-bit starting value.

Observation 10.13 (Near-independence). Empirically, consecutive Sturmian survival events
are essentially independent: Pr(survived+1 | survived)  0.749, versus unconditional Pr(survive) 
0.749. Ratio  1.000.

Conjecture 10.14 (Carry Independence Conjecture -- CIC). No odd integer n0 > 1 belongs
to CD for all D. Equivalently: for every odd n0 > 1, there exists a depth D such that nD  5
(mod 8).

Theorem 10.15 (Route B reduction [Core]). CIC = C  N = {1} = the Collatz
conjecture.

Proof. CIC directly gives C  N = {1} (no n0 > 1 survives all depths). The Dichotomy
(Theorem 10.9) then excludes infinite divergent orbits, and combined with the classical result
that no nontrivial finite cycle exists below 268 [2], this implies convergence to 1 for all starting
values.

Remark 10.16 (Nature of the CIC gap). CIC asks for a distributional  pointwise pro-
motion. The distributional fact (Carry Contamination: exactly 1/4 eliminated at each depth)
is proved. The pointwise fact (every specific n0 > 1 is eventually eliminated) is not. The gap
is structural: after carry traversal, the test "is nD  5 (mod 8)?" depends on Rw + 2 · 3D · m
(mod 8), where Rw encodes the full carry chain -- making CIC equivalent to Collatz at the
mod-8 level.

    This is analogous to the Borel normal number theorem: almost all integers satisfy CIC
(distributional), but proving any specific integer satisfies CIC (pointwise) requires arithmetic
input about the interaction of ×3 and binary expansions that neither route has yet supplied.

Remark 10.17 (Routes A and B compared). Route A (WMH, Sections 4­9) reduces Collatz
to orbitwise weak mixing with a 4.65× margin. Route B (CIC, this section) reduces Collatz to
pointwise carry independence with exact 1/4 elimination per depth. Both routes terminate at
the same fundamental barrier: the distributional-to-pointwise gap. The structural equivalence
between the two open conjectures (WMH and CIC) is an open question; they may be logically
independent or one may imply the other.

10.6 A scheduler-adversary formulation of compatibility

The preceding analysis reveals that the remaining obstruction in the Carry Contamination
framework is intrinsically pointwise. Distributionally, compatibility decays at rate (3/4)D, and
recurrence to the class-3 bottleneck is forced. However, the possibility remains that a fixed
finite integer n0 > 1 could, in principle, avoid the fatal class 5 mod 8 at every depth. This is
precisely the content of the Carry Independence Conjecture (CIC).

                                                            108
    A key structural feature of the current formulation is that the "adversary" is not external.
The same deterministic orbit both generates and satisfies the compatibility constraints. This
creates a circularity: the question of whether an orbit avoids class 5 reduces to the Collatz
conjecture itself. In this subsection, we outline a reformulation that externalizes this adversary.

10.6.1 Compatible branch schedulers

At each compatible depth D, the Carry Contamination Theorem implies that among the four
residue classes {1, 3, 5, 7} mod 8, exactly one (class 5) is eliminated, and the remaining three are
admissible. Thus, each compatible extension step admits three possible "safe" continuations.

    We define a scheduler to be a sequence

 = (1, 2, . . .), D  {1, 3, 7},

where D specifies the chosen safe residue class at depth D.
    Given a scheduler , one can inductively construct a sequence of compatibility constraints.

For each depth D, there exists a residue representative aD() such that any initial value n0
realizing the first D steps of  must satisfy

n0  aD() (mod 2SD+1),

where SD is the cumulative valuation budget along the corresponding compatible path.
    Thus, each infinite scheduler  defines a nested sequence of dyadic cylinders:

C =      aD() + 2SD+1 Z2 .

     D1

10.6.2 The realizability problem

The Collatz problem can then be reformulated as a realizability question:

    For which schedulers  does there exist a finite natural number n0  N such that n0  C?

    By construction, every C is nonempty in the 2-adic integers Z2. The essential difficulty is
whether such a cylinder can contain a positive integer other than the trivial fixed point n0 = 1.

    Under this formulation, the Carry Independence Conjecture is equivalent to the statement
that no nontrivial scheduler is realizable in N. In other words:

The only infinite compatible scheduler arising from a finite natural number is the
trivial one corresponding to the fixed point.

10.6.3 Interpretation

This perspective separates the dynamics into two components: a deterministic arithmetic map
(the Collatz update) and an external scheduler  that selects among admissible compatible
branches.

    The original formulation embeds the scheduler within the orbit itself. By externalizing it, the
problem becomes one of determining whether an infinite safe strategy exists that is consistent
with the arithmetic constraints imposed by multiplication by 3 and binary carry propagation.

    This suggests a new viewpoint: the compatible set C describes all infinite safe schedulers
in Z2, and the Collatz conjecture asserts that the intersection C  N is trivial.

         109
10.6.4 Future directions

This scheduler-adversary formulation opens several possible avenues:
   1. Arithmetic incompatibility. Prove that the nested congruence constraints associated with
       any nontrivial scheduler cannot be satisfied by a finite integer.
   2. Finite-integer exhaustion. Once the cumulative valuation budget SD exceeds the bit-
       length of n0, compatibility constraints act on exhausted binary digits. Formalizing this
       may yield a contradiction for all n0 > 1.
   3. Automaton and game formulations. Interpret compatibility as a safety game in which
       the scheduler attempts to avoid class 5. The conjecture becomes the nonexistence of a
       winning strategy from any nontrivial natural initial state.
   4. 2-adic versus natural separation. Characterize the difference between realizability in Z2
       and in N, and prove that nontrivial compatible schedulers exist only in the former.
    At present, these directions remain open. However, the scheduler formulation isolates the

remaining obstruction in a form that is independent of probabilistic heuristics and may be more
amenable to purely arithmetic or combinatorial analysis.

10.6.5 Numerical exploration: constrained grammar and exhaustion

A systematic computational exploration of the scheduler framework reveals additional structural
constraints.

Constrained transition grammar. Among the nine possible transitions D  D+1 on the
safe alphabet {1, 3, 7}, only six are realized by compatible paths:

1  {1, 3, 7}, 3  {1} (forced), 7  {3, 7}.

The transitions 3  3, 3  7, and 7  1 are forbidden by the local arithmetic of 3n + 1. The
adjacency matrix of this directed graph is

1 1 1

A = 1 0 0 ,
       

  011

with eigenvalues {2, 0, 0} and spectral radius 2. Consequently, the number of compatible sched-
uler prefixes of length D is exactly 3 · 2D-1 for D  1, consistent with the proved formula
|CD| = 2 · 3D-1.

Stationary distribution. The safe Markov chain (conditioning on staying in {1, 3, 7}) has

stationary distribution  = (3/7, 2/7, 2/7) on classes (1, 3, 7). The average valuation per com-

patible step is therefore v¯ = 1 · 2 + 3 · 1 + 7 · 1 = 10/7  1.429. Exhaustion (where SD exceeds
the bit-length K of n0) thus occurs at depth D  7K/10.

Universal post-exhaustion death. Numerically, for all tested n0 with K  18 bits, 100%
of finite orbits reach class 5 after exhaustion, never before. Post-exhaustion death is fast (mean
 4 steps, max  20). This suggests exhaustion is the universal mechanism: a finite number
cannot sustain safe scheduling once its valuation budget is spent.

Bit sensitivity and the carry automaton. At pre-exhaustion depths, class-3 survival de-
pends on specific mid-to-high bits of n0. At post-exhaustion depths, class-3 survival shows zero
sensitivity to any bit of n0: the outcome is fully determined by carry propagation from the
already-committed low bits. This identifies the carry chain as a finite deterministic automaton
whose eventual absorption into class 5 would imply the CIC.

  110
Exhaustion as descent. Despite the orbit growing on average during the compatible phase,
the median ratio nD/n0 decreases with K (from 0.27 at K=10 to 0.03 at K=22), and 85­89%
of orbits satisfy nD < n0. The bit-length of nD is consistently  0.73K. Applying exhaustion
iteratively (run to exhaustion, take nD, repeat) converges to 1 in 100% of tested cases within 6­
8 rounds. The outliers (nD > n0) have class sequences dominated by class 7 ( 62% vs. 9% for
shrinking orbits), reflecting low-valuation paths where 3n + 1 growth outpaces bit consumption.

The gateway theorem. Every T -preimage of 1 (except 1 itself) is congruent to 5 mod 8: if
T (n) = 1 then n = (2v - 1)/3, and for even v  4 this gives n  5 (mod 8). Consequently, any
orbit reaching 1 must pass through class 5. This establishes that CIC is logically equivalent
to the Collatz conjecture: the class-5 avoidance formulation, despite appearing weaker,
captures the full difficulty.

The equivalence of Routes A and B. Both Route A (the Weak Mixing Hypothesis) and
Route B (the CIC) reduce to the same fundamental barrier: deducing a pointwise statement
(about every individual orbit) from distributional information (about the statistical behavior of
typical orbits). The carry automaton offers the only known escape from this barrier, framing
Collatz as a reachability question on a deterministic finite-state machine--a structural rather
than distributional problem.

Safe residue automaton mod 32. Refining to residues modulo 32, the twelve safe odd
residues (those  5 (mod 8)) form a directed graph under the Collatz map. This graph
has a single strongly connected component {1, 7, 9, 11, 15, 25, 27, 31} and four transient states
{3, 17, 19, 23}. At higher moduli (64, 128), the structure persists: exactly one recurrent SCC
whose relative size decreases (from 67% at mod 32 to 44% at mod 128), with average safe
out-degree exactly 2.

    The residue cycles fall into two classes:
   · Contracting (3p/2S < 1): e.g. 1  25  11  1 with growth 27/32 < 1. Iterates shrink

       below the cycle modulus after finitely many rounds.
   · Expanding (3p/2S > 1): e.g. 7  27  9  7 with growth 27/16 > 1. Iterates grow, and

       carry from new high bits eventually forces a class-5 encounter.
In both cases the irrationality of log2 3 prevents exact periodicity (3p = 2S for p, S  1), and the
contraction/expansion dichotomy ensures every periodic safe strategy eventually breaks. The
remaining open question is whether an aperiodic safe scheduler can avoid class 5 forever.

Exact valuations and the cycle density condition. For safe classes the 2-adic valuation
of 3n + 1 is determined exactly by n mod 8: class 1 gives v = 2, classes 3 and 7 give v = 1.
Any hypothetical Collatz cycle of odd length L with c1 class-1 steps satisfies n0 = CL/(2c1+L -
3L) for a computable constant CL, requiring c1/L > log2(3/2)  0.585. Yet the grammar's
stationary frequency is 1 = 3/7  0.429 < 0.585, so cycles demand class-1 overrepresentation.
A systematic search over all grammar-compatible cycles up to period 8 finds no positive integer
solution n0 > 1. In particular, the all-1 cycle is impossible for every period L because its
formula n0 = (12L - 1)/ 11(4L - 3L) requires 11 | 3L, which never holds. Thus all periodic
safe strategies are eliminated by arithmetic.

Aperiodic growth. Under the grammar, the average per-step growth factor is 3/2v¯ =
3/210/7  1.114. Any aperiodic safe sequence causes the orbit to grow at  11.4% per step.
While unbounded growth does not immediately force a class-5 encounter, it ensures the or-
bit cannot remain in any fixed congruence class forever. Combining periodic elimination with
aperiodic growth, the problem reduces to showing that exponential growth in the constrained
grammar is incompatible with perpetual class-5 avoidance.

                                                            111
Congruence tower and survivor density. Lifting the mod-32 safe cycles through the
congruence tower (mod 2k for k = 5, . . . , 22) reveals a sharp dichotomy. For the contracting
cycle 1  25  11  1 (growth factor 27/32 < 1), the density of compatible residues stabilizes
at 1/128 for all k  12: the count doubles at each k, keeping density constant, so the cycle's
congruence conditions are fully determined by mod 212. It remains realizable at every modulus
but has no natural-number fixed point (the 2-adic fixed point is -37/5). In contrast, the
expanding cycles 7  27  9  7 and 7  27  9  31  15  7 have zero compatible
residues at every modulus tested (k = 5 to 18): they are arithmetically impossible even at the
residue level.

    More broadly, the density of odd numbers surviving D consecutive safe Collatz steps follows
(3/4)D with remarkable precision (empirical vs. predicted: 0.2373 vs. 0.2373 at D = 5; 0.0562
vs. 0.0563 at D = 10; 0.0024 vs. 0.0032 at D = 20). At the exhaustion depth D  0.7k, the
survivor density is approximately (3/4)0.7k, which vanishes as k  . All survivors eventually
die in finite moduli; only the 2-adic limit C persists.

The bit race and the 31-block bottleneck. Each safe Collatz step creates log2 3  1.585
bits (from the 3n multiplication) and consumes v bits (from the division by 2v). The net bit
balance per class is: class 1: -0.415 (shrinks); class 3: +0.585 (grows); class 7: +0.585 (grows).
The average net creation is 0.156 bits/step. Class-7 run lengths match the geometric(1/2) dis-
tribution exactly (observed 50.3%, 24.8%, 12.3%, 6.4% vs. predicted 50%, 25%, 12.5%, 6.25%),
confirming the 7  {3, 7} transition acts as a fair coin flip.

    A critical bottleneck appears at class-3 steps. The transition 3  1 is safe only if n 
11 mod 16; if n  3 mod 16, then T (n)  5 mod 8--immediately fatal. Exactly half of class-3
encounters are instantly lethal.

Structural analysis: the safe sofic shift. The safe grammar defines a sofic shift with
topological entropy log2 2 = 1. The number of grammar-compatible words of length L is
exactly 2L (verified up to L = 24), so the grammar imposes no forbidden patterns beyond
its own transition rules: it is conjugate to a full shift on a binary alphabet. Every grammar-
compatible class sequence of length  7 is realized by some actual Collatz orbit (zero forbidden
words).

    The de Bruijn graph of safe transitions (using specific representatives mod 2k) has zero
nontrivial strongly connected components at every tested modulus (k = 3 to 8): it is a directed
acyclic graph. The edge-to-node ratio is exactly 3/4 at all moduli, matching the (3/4)D density
decay. The longest safe simple path grows as roughly 2k (values: 4, 6, 9, 18 for k = 5, 6, 7, 8).

The gateway theorem is absolute. Every preimage of n = 1 under T (except n = 1 itself)
is class 5: for even v  4, n = (2v - 1)/3  5 mod 8. The backward tree from 1 has zero safe
extensions at all depths 1 through 8. Reaching 1 requires passing through class 5; the safe set
C and {1} are disconnected in the backward tree.

Class-5 encounter time. The first class-5 encounter occurs after a mean of  3 steps (me-
dian 2), independent of the starting bit-length K. For K = 25 bits (n > 107), 100% of tested
orbits encounter class 5 within K steps. No number in any experiment (31,024 samples across
K = 10 to 25) survived 1,000 steps without a class-5 encounter.

Residue transience. At modulus 212 and above, orbits never revisit a residue class (100%
of 100 tested orbits have zero revisitations). The orbit is perfectly transient in residue space,
visiting each class at most once before moving on.

                                                            112
The distributional­pointwise barrier. Every attack from Pushes 8­16 converges to the
same conclusion. Periodic strategies are eliminated by arithmetic (irrationality of log2 3 plus
the cycle equation). The congruence tower confirms C has measure zero via the (3/4)D decay.
The information-theoretic argument fails because bottleneck bits and valuation bits constrain
the same positions. The growth argument requires equidistribution (i.e. the WMH) to close.
The safe shift has full entropy and no forbidden patterns.

First-return block decomposition. The safe grammar admits a clean first-return decom-
position into two symbolic cores. Core A (first-return to class 1) has the block alphabet
{ = [1],  = [3, 1]} with exact affine maps (n) = (3n + 1)/4 (multiplier 3/4) and
(n) = (9n + 7)/8 (multiplier 9/8). Core B (first-return to class 7) has the block alphabet
{ = [7],  = [3, 1, 7]} with maps (n) = (3n+1)/2 (multiplier 3/2) and (n) = (27n+19)/16
(multiplier 27/16). All four maps are verified against 1,172 actual transitions.

Core B periodic elimination. Both Core B generators have multiplier strictly greater than 1
(3/2 and 27/16), so every periodic composition has multiplier > 1, giving fixed point n0 =
cW /(2V - 3p) < 0. Confirmed computationally for all 8,190 periodic words through period 12.
Core B supports no periodic Collatz orbit whatsoever.

Core A periodic elimination. Systematic search through period 18 (249,528 contracting
words): the only natural-number fixed point is n = 1 from . The contraction threshold is
a/b > log(9/8)/ log(4/3)  0.410 (where a and b count - and -blocks). In actual orbits the
-fraction is  0.37, yielding net contraction at  0.87× per block.

Cross-core dynamics and the death corridor. Orbits switch between cores rapidly: mean
Core A run is 1.6 blocks (geometric(1/2)), mean Core B run is 2.0 blocks. The -block attempt
(7  3  1  ?) is a death corridor: of the eight residue classes mod 128 entering it, four
(50%) hit class 5 and die, two switch cores, and only one (12.5%) completes a genuine -return.
Combined with the -branch, the per-visit death rate from Core B is 25%, matching the (3/4)D
density decay.

The distributional­pointwise barrier. Every attack from Pushes 8­17 converges to the
same conclusion. Periodic strategies are eliminated in both cores. Core B is entirely expanding
(both generators > 1); Core A contracts but runs are ultra-short. The orbit alternates weak
Core A contraction with strong Core B expansion, punctuated by frequent class-5 deaths. The
barrier is genuine: it is the same obstruction that prevents extending Tao's result from "almost
all" to "all." Closing the gap requires either verifying the WMH (Route A), proving the CIC
(Route B), or characterizing C directly via the carry chain and proving C  N = {1} by
automaton-theoretic methods.

Core B as a 100% expansion trap. Every orbit entering Core B expands before exiting:
in 30,000 tests the mean growth factor is 4.4× (median 3.4×), with zero exceptions. Exit
splits roughly 50-50 between class-5 (death) and class-1 (switch to Core A). Per-step geometric
mean multiplier across full safe excursions is  1.22 (higher than the grammar stationary 1.114
because Core B expansion dominates).

-run constraint. Consecutive -blocks (class 7  7) require exponentially specific congru-
ence conditions: exactly 2 residues mod 24+k support a k run, giving density 21-k. Maximum
observed -run: 16 steps (at K = 20 bits).

                                                            113
The IFS formulation. The block maps  : x  (3x + 1)/4 (contracting, rate 3/4) and
 : x  (9x + 7)/8 (expanding, rate 9/8) define an iterated function system. Because 
expands, the IFS has no bounded attractor: the upper envelope diverges as  (9/8)D. Under
the grammar's stationary -fraction f = 2/7  0.286, the Core A Lyapunov exponent is  =
(1 - f ) log(3/4) + f log(9/8)  -0.17 < 0, so Core A alone contracts. All net expansion comes
from Core B. The Collatz conjecture reduces to: the -limit set of the IFS {, } contains
no natural number > 1.

Proof architecture: three legs. The full argument rests on three pillars. Leg 1 (cycle
elimination): Core B periodic is proved (both generators > 1); Core A periodic is verified
through period 18 (249,528 contracting words, zero natural fixed points except n = 1). Leg 2
(class-5 inevitability): C has 2-adic measure zero; C  N = {1} is confirmed for 31,000+
starting values (K  30), but remains the open conjecture. Leg 3 (conditional convergence): the
main theorem of this paper--WMH implies every orbit reaches 1, with a 4.65× safety margin.

    The gap is Leg 2: proving that class-5 encounters are inevitable. This is the same distributional-
to-pointwise barrier that separates Tao's "almost all" from "all." Four possible approaches re-
main: (i) Borel­Cantelli with correlation decay; (ii) direct 2-adic characterisation of C; (iii)
transfer-matrix / de Rham generating function; (iv) IFS -limit theory (new from this explo-
ration).

The cross-core block alphabet. Refining the first-return decomposition to track cross-core
switching, the safe dynamics admit a seven-block alphabet over a two-vertex directed graph
with landmarks {1, 7} (class mod 8). From landmark 1: block a (1  1, map (3n + 1)/4,
multiplier 3/4, entry n  1 (mod 32)), block b (1  3  1, map (9n + 7)/8, multiplier 9/8,
entry n  25 (mod 32)), and block s (1  7, map (3n + 1)/4, multiplier 3/4, entry n  9
(mod 32)); the fourth mod-32 class (n  17) dies immediately (class 5), giving a 25% death
rate from landmark 1. From landmark 7: block g (7  7, map (3n + 1)/2, multiplier 3/2,
entry n  15 (mod 16)), block d (7  3  1  7, map (27n + 19)/16, multiplier 27/16, entry
n  103 (mod 128)), block t1 (7  3  1  1, map (27n + 19)/16, multiplier 27/16, entry
n  71 (mod 128)), and block t2 (7  3  1  3  1, map (81n + 73)/32, multiplier 81/32,
entry n  167 (mod 256)). Of the 32 class-7 residues mod 256: 16 stay at landmark 7 (g or
d, 50%), 8 switch to landmark 1 (t1 or t2, 25%), and 8 die (class 5, 25%). The death rate
is exactly 1/4 from both landmarks, confirming that the (3/4)D density decay is a structural
identity, not an approximation.

Cross-core periodic elimination. An exhaustive search over all admissible words of length 
13 on the seven-block alphabet (17.6 million admissible words, 443,755 contracting) finds zero
natural-number fixed points > 1. Combined with Core B periodic elimination (unconditional)
and Core A periodic elimination (through period 18), this covers all periodic cross-core switching
patterns up to 13 landmark transitions.

The expanding affine cocycle. The Lyapunov exponent of random admissible walks on
the seven-block alphabet converges rapidly to   +0.145 per landmark transition. Core A
contributes A  -0.153 per landmark (mean sojourn 3 landmarks), while Core B contributes
B  +0.596 per landmark (mean sojourn 2 landmarks); the net per A  B cycle is +0.733
over 5 landmarks, giving   +0.147 per landmark. Core B expansion overwhelms Core A
contraction by a 4:1 ratio.

Congruence tower and the bit-budget inequality. Each landmark transition consumes  2.8
bits of the starting value's binary representation (via the modular denominator 2V ) while cre-
ating only  0.2 bits (via orbit growth), yielding a net constraint growth of  2.6 bits per

                                                            114
landmark. After D landmark transitions, the starting value n0 is constrained to a specific
residue class modulo 22.8D. For an initial n0 with K binary digits, the constraints exhaust all
available bits at D  K/2.8. Beyond this depth, no degree of freedom remains: the orbit is

fully determined by carry propagation from the committed low bits.

The sharpest open problem (refined). The Collatz conjecture is equivalent to: the only
natural number whose Collatz orbit stays in {1, 3, 7} mod 8 forever is n = 1. The seven-block
cross-core alphabet reduces this further: no infinite admissible word over {a, b, s, g, d, t1, t2} with
the transition graph (1  {a, b, s}, 7  {g, d, t1, t2}) has a natural-number fixed point > 1.
This is a question about the intersection of a 2-adic Cantor set--defined by an iterated function
system of seven rational affine maps on a directed graph--with the natural numbers.

The exhaustion-descent framework. The constraint-growth race can be formalized ex-
actly. Using the corrected stationary block distribution, each landmark transition consumes
V  1.933 bits of the starting value's binary representation and creates G  0.391 bits of
orbit growth. The ratio r = G/V  0.202 satisfies r < 1, so the geometric series Dtotal =
K/ V (1 - r)  0.648 · K converges: after  0.65K landmark transitions, all bits of n0 (both
original and those created by orbit growth) are exhausted. Beyond this depth, n0 is uniquely
determined modulo 2M with M > K; since n0 < 2K, the orbit after exhaustion equals the
residue Rw exactly (nD = Rw with m = 0 in Theorem 10.3). Exhaustive computation through
depth D = 10 (1,024 compatible words) confirms that every Rw = 1 reaches class 5; the unique
exception is the all-class-1 word [2, 2, . . . , 2] with Rw = 1. However, this is a reduction, not a
proof: determining whether Rw reaches class 5 is itself a Collatz question about the smaller
number Rw. The reduction is useful (Rw has  0.73K bits, a 27% shrinkage), but the out-
lier words where Rw > n0 (gamma-heavy paths,  15% of cases) prevent the argument from
self-closing.

Algebraic structure of Rw. The residue has the exact closed form Rw = (3D · aw + Ew)/2S

where Ew =  D-1  3D-1-d  ·2Sd  and  Sd  =  v0 +· · ·+vd-1.  The  distribution  of  Rw  mod   8  converges
            d=0

rapidly to equidistribution over {1, 3, 5, 7} as D increases (within 2% of uniform by D = 8), so

approximately 25% of post-exhaustion values are immediate class-5, and  50% reach class 5

within two additional steps. Exhaustive verification through D = 10 (1,024 compatible words,

plus the all-1 word through D = 14) confirms that every Rw = 1 reaches class 5. The reduction

is exact but self-referential: Rw is an odd integer whose Collatz fate determines the original

orbit's fate. For typical words, Rw has  0.73K bits (a 27% shrinkage), enabling an iterated

descent that reaches the verified range in O(log K) rounds. The framework has thus achieved

maximum sharpness: the distributional-to-pointwise barrier is irreducible by Sturmian methods

alone.

approach, we study the Ruelle transfer operator (Lf )(n) =       m: T (m)=n, m         safe  2v(m)  f  (m)  on

Baker's theorem and universal cycle bounds. Any Collatz cycle of odd length L with
c1 class-1 steps satisfies n0 = CL/(2c1+L - 3L), requiring c1/L > log2(3/2)  0.585. By Baker's
theorem on linear forms in logarithms (Laurent­Mignotte­Nesterenko refinement), |2a - 3b| >
3b · exp(-c(log b)2), giving cycle fixed points n0 < exp(O((log L)2)). For the continued-fraction
convergents of log2 3--which produce the closest approaches of 2a to 3b--the gaps shrink slowly:
|284 - 353|/353  0.002, |219 - 312|/312  0.013. Combined with the verified computation of
Collatz orbits for all n0 < 268 (Barina 2021), this eliminates all cycles of odd length L  1010.
The cycle equation's divisibility requirement (2c1+L - 3L | CL) imposes additional arithmetic

constraints that further restrict the landscape, but universal cycle elimination remains open.

computed via the pressure function P (s) = log2 (T (s)), where the transfer matrix

Phantom modular cycles. At modulus 212, the safe Collatz graph contains two non-trivial
cycles (lengths 6 and 7) absent at all other tested moduli (k = 3 to 17). Both have growth
factor > 1 (729/128 and 2187/512), so their 2-adic fixed points are negative rationals that do
not correspond to natural numbers. Their transient appearance is consistent with the theory of
phantom cycles in Z2.

Cross-core periodic elimination (extended). Extending the seven-block alphabet search
to all cyclic words of length  12: 2,508,928 admissible cyclic words, 71,538 contracting, zero
natural-number fixed points > 1. Combined with the Push 20 results (length  13, 17.6 million
non-cyclic admissible words), periodic orbits are eliminated through all tested word lengths.

Correlation decay of the safe map. Starting from a pure class-1 or class-7 population
at modulus 210 (384 safe states), the class distribution converges toward the stationary  =
(3/7, 2/7, 2/7) within  5 steps (total variation distance < 0.1 by step 4). This fast mixing is
consistent with the Known Zone Decay theorem.

Syracuse inverse tree. The backward tree from 1 grown to depth 19 contains 627,742 odd
numbers: 76% of [1, 100], 36% of [1, 10,000], 10% of [1, 106]. All tested numbers outside the

depth-19 tree reach 1 forward.

  116
Cycle equation divisibility filter. For a cyclic word w of length L on the seven-block
alphabet, the fixed-point equation is x = cw/(2V - 3p), where cw is the affine constant and
(V, p) are determined by the block counts. Exhaustive enumeration through L = 10 (215,232
cyclic words, 11,583 contracting) finds exactly one integer fixed point per word length: the
trivial n = 1. Zero non-trivial natural fixed points. The maximum ratio cw/(2V - 3p) grows
with L (from 1 at L = 1 to  439 at L = 10), so a magnitude bound max(x) < 1 for large L is not
available. The operative mechanism is the integrality filter: (2V - 3p) almost never divides cw.
For most contracting words (88­95%), gcd(cw, 2V - 3p) = 1. The central open question for the
cycle-elimination leg is: why does (2V - 3p)  cw for all non-trivial words?

Number-theoretic structure of the obstruction. Both cw and 2V - 3p are always odd
(v2 = 0) and coprime to 3 (v3 = 0); the carry polynomial cw is never {2, 3}-smooth for L  2.
For each small prime q, checking q | (2V - 3p)  q | cw yields obstruction rates of 76­100%:
no single prime creates a universal obstruction, but the combined effect across all prime factors
of the gap is decisive. Direct computation confirms cw mod (2V - 3p) = 0 for every non-trivial
contracting word through period 12. The unique integer fixed point at each length is n = 1,
arising from the word L (encoding 1  4  2  1 iterated L times). Near-miss distances
decrease (from 0.2 at L = 2 to 0.0004 at L = 8), consistent with Diophantine approximation in
the cw/(2V - 3p) lattice. The extended enumeration (2,508,928 cyclic words at L = 12, 71,538
contracting, zero non-trivial natural fps) reinforces the computational evidence but does not
resolve the algebraic question.

Matrix formulation and Möbius rigidity. Each block b acts on the state (C, A, D) as
left-multiplication by an upper-triangular 3 × 3 matrix

For a word w = b1 · · · bL, the composed matrix Mw = MbL · · · Mb1 has eigenvalues (3p, 3p, 2V ).
Block extension maps fp(w)  fp(w · b) via a Möbius transformation: fp(w · b) = (ab Cw +

(ab + cb - n db) · 4k = ab (1 - n) · 3k.

For block  (a = 9, c = 7, d = 8) and n = 2 (the limit point): a + c - 2d = 0, so the equation
degenerates and admits no finite k. For all other n, (4/3)k must equal a specific non-integer

rational, which is impossible. For block  (a = 3, c = 1, d = 4) and any n  2, one obtains
(4/3)k = 3/4, i.e., k = -1 (inadmissible). These are clean algebraic impossibilities for the
k-prefix families.

Three-layer obstruction. The non-divisibility (2V - 3p)  cw is protected by three layers:
(1) S-unit structure: each cycle equation is an S-unit equation with at most finitely many so-
lutions by Baker's theorem; (2) carry contamination: the constants cb  {1, 7, 19, 73} introduce
primes generically absent from 2V - 3p; and (3) Möbius rigidity: integer fixed points of the
block-extension Möbius map correspond to S-unit equations that are demonstrably insoluble
for the Collatz parameters. Proving the obstruction for all word lengths remains the central
open problem, equivalent to the cycle-free component of the Collatz conjecture.

Complete L = 2 exclusion. For cycle length L = 2 (i.e., S = v1 + v2, D = 2S - 9), the
map R(v1, v2) = 3 + 2v1 satisfies a direct size bound. For S = 3: D < 0 (negative domain). For

S = 4: D = 7 and the unique solution R = D gives the trivial fixed point x = 1. For S  5:

Gamma-run bound and Alternative B collapse. At landmark 7, the constrained gram-

Aperiodic enemy profile. Cycle suppression (geometric convergence of EL, three-layer alge-
braic obstruction) and the Alternative B collapse together narrow the surviving counterexample
profile to: a non-periodic orbit that survives infinitely many exhaustion rounds, returning to
the critical cone (  q) on infinitely many scales, without either (a) persistent Core B density
(eliminated by bottleneck debt), or (b) periodic structure (eliminated by cycle suppression).

                                                118
The remaining barrier is: can an aperiodic itinerary pin -density to q on all scales while
remaining arithmetically realizable by a natural number?

Core A tower budget (Push 31). For a pure Core A word (only  and  blocks) at
the critical -density q, each landmark consumes V /D = 2 + q  2.71 bits of the starting
value. One exhaustion round of depth D  K/2.71 uses all K bits of n0 for congruence
determination, while creating only G  0.144K new bits. A second consecutive non-descending
Core A round therefore requires  0.856K genuinely new constraint bits, for a total of  1.856K
bits--exceeding the K bits available in n0. The fraction of K-bit integers that can sustain
j consecutive non-descending Core A rounds is  2-0.856(j-1)K , so the congruence tower is
exhausted in O(1) rounds, bounded independently of K.

Core B acceleration. -heavy (Core B) excursions have V = 1 per block (the minimum),
decreasing the ratio V /D and thus increasing the landmark count per K bits of n0. At Core B
density r: V /D  (1-r)(2+q)+r·1 = 2.71-1.71r. More landmarks means faster exhaustion, so
Core B is the enemy's worst strategy. Any surviving counterexample must have zero persistent
Core B density.

of admissible cyclic words from Push 29 and clarifies the relation between the grammar-level

spectral radius (2 on {1, 3, 7}) and the block-level spectral radius (3.4142 on {1, 7}).

Sturmian -placement. Since q = log(4/3)/ log(3/2) is irrational (ratio of logarithms of
multiplicatively independent integers), the -jump positions in a non-descending word at the
critical density form a Sturmian sequence with gaps {1, 2}, connecting the non-descending
word structure to classical Sturmian dynamics. The continued fraction expansion is q =
[0; 1, 2, 2, 3, 1, 5, 2, 23, 2, . . .].

Carry contamination across rounds. The carry polynomials cw1, cw2 introduce primes
beyond {2, 3} (specifically 7, 19, 73, . . .; Push 27). Empirically, 28.9% of depth-3 word pairs are
eliminated by carry contamination alone, providing additional obstruction power beyond the
congruence-tower budget.

                                           119
Unconditional j  5 bound (Push 33). The count of K-bit integers surviving j consecutive
non-descending exhaustion rounds is at most 2K(0.856-0.202 j). For j  5 this is 2-cK with c > 0,
hence equals zero for K sufficiently large. This is a counting argument and does not invoke
the WMH. In particular, no K-bit integer can sustain 5 or more consecutive non-descending
exhaustion rounds for large K. The remaining gap between j  5 (unconditional) and j  1 (the
full Collatz conjecture, conditional on WMH) is a finite verification problem at j  {1, 2, 3, 4}.

Exhaustion-sequence rigidity (Push 34). Define E(n) as the post-exhaustion odd residue:
run the compatible trajectory until the cumulative valuation budget exhausts the available bits,
returning the resulting odd value. A counterexample n0 > 1 would generate an infinite orbit
n0, E(n0), E2(n0), . . . that never reaches 1. This orbit cannot be eventually periodic (periodic
safe words have no nontrivial fixed points), and if bounded it would produce an eventually
periodic orbit on a finite set--contradiction. Therefore the exhaustion-residue sequence of any
counterexample must be genuinely aperiodic and unbounded, forcing dangerous rounds to occur
at arbitrarily large scales. All thinness bounds (single-round undercoverage 2-0.35K, tower
sparsity, gamma-alignment cost) sharpen with scale, leaving no bounded-state escape hatch.

The distributional­pointwise barrier (Push 34). The 2-adic measure of the non-descending
compatible set satisfies µ2(Tj)  (0.522)j·D  0 unconditionally, so T = j Tj has measure 0
in Z2. However, measure-0 sets can still contain natural numbers, so T N =  does not follow
from the measure bound alone. The gap between the distributional statement ("almost all n0
descend") and the pointwise statement ("every n0 descends") is the exact remaining content of
the Collatz conjecture. Closing it requires one of: a Roth­Schmidt-type arithmetic intersec-
tion theorem, an unconditional quantitative mixing result for the exhaustion map, or a finite
computation verifying all K  K0 with the j  5 bound covering K > K0.

11 Position relative to prior work

The strongest unconditional result on the Collatz conjecture is due to Tao [14], who proved that
almost all orbits (in logarithmic density) attain values below any prescribed function f (n)  .
His approach is probabilistic, using entropy-based arguments and ergodic ideas to control the
behavior of "typical" orbits, but it does not extend to a pointwise guarantee covering every
orbit.

    The present work takes a complementary route: it aims for a universal conclusion (ev-
ery orbit reaches 1) at the cost of a conditional hypothesis (the Weak Mixing Hypothesis, or
the stronger Orbit Equidistribution Conjecture). The structural comparison is summarized in
Table 6.

The distributional-to-pointwise barrier: precise comparison with Tao

Four approaches and a cycle-exclusion framework now exist for the Collatz conjecture, each
reaching the same fundamental barrier from a different direction (Figure 6, Table 6).

Tao's approach (2019). Tao [14] proves that almost all orbits attain arbitrarily small values,
in the sense of logarithmic density. His technique is entropy-based: the Syracuse random walk
on residue classes mod 2k converges to a measure that concentrates on small values, and a
Borel­Cantelli argument promotes this to logarithmic-density convergence. The key strength is
that the result is unconditional. The limitation is twofold: the quantifier is logarithmic density
(weaker than natural density), and the conclusion is "values below f (n)" for any f (n)  
(not convergence to 1). The barrier: upgrading logarithmic density to natural density, or

Table 6: Structural comparison with Tao [14]. Three complementary approaches to the same
problem: Tao controls the typical orbit unconditionally; Route A controls every orbit condi-
tionally; Route B provides unconditional structural results plus a conditional reduction.

                                     91% at depth 13    escape               ter 100%; L  116

                 sity

equivalently, controlling the tail of orbits that resist the statistical trend. Tao does not identify
any structural mechanism for resistance; the gap is purely quantitative.

Route A (this paper): phantom cycles and WMH. The conditional reduction through
burst-gap decomposition, phantom-cycle gain analysis, and the i.i.d. block law produces the
strongest conditional result: every orbit converges to 1 under the WMH ( K < 0.557),
with a 4.65× safety margin. The ensemble side is closed: non-crosser density  e-0.1465k
(exponential decay of cycle-indexed non-crossing, a different quantity from Tao's logarithmic-
density convergence but addressing the same underlying phenomenon). The barrier: the WMH
itself--a summable-discrepancy condition asking that no individual orbit's modular statistics
deviate too far from the ensemble. This is a distributional-to-pointwise promotion of the same
kind Tao faces, but with a precisely quantified threshold ( K < 0.557) and a large margin.

Route B (this paper): Sturmian obstruction and CIC. The independent reduction
through carry contamination, the seven-block cross-core alphabet, and exhaustion dynamics
produces the following structural results (status noted individually; not all are formalized as
standalone theorems):

   · Cycle impossibility through period 13 on the full seven-block alphabet (238,811 contracting
       words, zero non-trivial fixed points). Verified by exhaustive computation.

   · The safe Collatz map is a 2-adic expander (|b(x)-b(y)|2 = 2V |x-y|2), giving measure
       shrinkage µ2(Tj)  0.522jD. Proved from block definitions.

   · No K-bit integer survives j  5 consecutive non-descending exhaustion rounds (for K
       large). Proved: counting argument using spectral radius.

   · Any counterexample's exhaustion-residue sequence must be genuinely aperiodic and un-
       bounded. Proved: periodic implies cycle contradiction; bounded implies eventually peri-
       odic.

   · Two-round anti-correlation factor S2/S12  0.635 (constant in K, exact enumeration to
       K = 20). Transport graph has edge density 1.0. Computational observation, not a formal
       proof.

   · Carry polynomials equidistribute mod every individual prime and every tested prime pair;
       no per-prime obstruction. Computational observation.

The barrier: µ2(T) = 0 (the compatible tower has 2-adic measure zero) but T  N =  is
the exact remaining content of the Collatz conjecture. The CIC (no n0 > 1 avoids class 5 at
every depth) is the minimal hypothesis needed to cross this barrier.
