Acyclicity of GM + means that the ranking function VM+ bounds not just single excursions but the
full above-start dynamics within the verified mixed-state model, including excursion­exit­return
chains.

A.7 Cycle genealogy and the birth obstruction

Tracking cycle lineage across modular depths reveals a clean pattern. Each cycle at depth M
either lifts from a cycle at M - 1 (its residues project to a cycle modulo 2M-1) or is born fresh
at level M .

Proposition A.12 (Cycle genealogy (computational)). For M = 7 through 18:

   1. Net-positive cycles are born at M = 10, 11, and 12, and die at M = 11, 12, and 13
       respectively (via the carry parity obstruction).

   2. No net-positive cycle is born at any M  13.

   3. The only cycle detected in the modular transition graph for M  13 (verified through
       M = 18) is the trivial length-1 fixed point r = 1 with v2 = 2 and negative drift.

    A further structural observation constrains the possibility of net-positive cycle formation at
high M :

                                                            149
Proposition A.13 (Positive-drift subgraph acyclicity (computational)). For all M from 10
through 18, the subgraph of the Syracuse transition graph restricted to positive-drift residues
(v2 = 1, i.e., r  3 (mod 4)) contains no cycles. The maximum chain length in this subgraph
equals exactly M .

Since net-positive cycles require a majority of positive-drift steps, and the purely positive-drift
subgraph is itself acyclic, any net-positive cycle must involve "detours" through negative-drift
residues. The carry parity obstruction shows that the resulting mixed-sign cycles at M = 10­12
cannot survive refinement, and no new such cycles form at higher depth.

A.8 The Net-Positive Ranking Lemma

Combining the above yields the main result of this section:

Lemma A.14 (Net-Positive Ranking). For M  13 (verified through M = 19), the net-positive
state graph GM is acyclic. The function VM (s) = length of the longest directed path from s to
an exit state satisfies

                                                VM (s)  VM (s) - 1

for every edge s  s in GM .

Corollary A.15 (Bounded above-start persistence in the modular graph). No trajectory of the
accelerated Syracuse map, when projected into the net-positive state graph at modular depth
M  13, can remain within that net-positive graph for more than maxs VM (s) steps. Empiri-
cally, maxs VM (s)  5M for K = 1 and  2M for K = 5, consistent with Known-Zone Decay
(Theorem 6.1). This is a statement about the modular model, not directly about full Collatz
orbits.

Remark A.16 (Layer 1 vs. Layer 2). Lemma A.14 resolves what we call Layer 1 within the
modular net-positive graph: each above-start excursion inside that graph is bounded by VM .

    Layer 2: the re-entry problem: asks whether an orbit can repeatedly exit the core, wander
through negative-drift states, and re-enter with restored budget. Proposition A.11 provides
strong computational evidence that the answer is no, at least within the verified mixed-state
model: the augmented return graph G+M remains acyclic for M = 13 through 17, and its ranking
function VM+ bounds the full above-start dynamics including excursion­exit­return chains. A
proof that GM + is acyclic for all M would resolve Layer 2 completely.

Conjecture A.17 (Augmented acyclicity for all M  13). For every M  13 and every K  1,
the augmented return graph GM + is acyclic.

The conjecture subsumes two claims: (a) no net-positive-drift cycles exist in the modular tran-
sition graph for M  13 (resolving Layer 1), and (b) re-entry edges from outside the core do not
create new cycles (resolving Layer 2). The carry parity obstruction (Proposition A.8) provides
the mechanism for (a): existing cycles cannot lift. The cycle genealogy (Proposition A.12)
shows that no new net-positive cycles are born at M  13. The augmented acyclicity (Propo-
sition A.11) verifies the full conjecture through M = 17.

    If Conjecture A.17 holds, then the ranking function VM+ applies at every sufficiently large
modular depth, bounding the full above-start dynamics (including re-entry) to at most O(M )
accelerated steps.

A.9 The exit-return reduction

The following structural theorems reduce the augmented acyclicity conjecture to a smaller
boundary problem. Let GC denote the core graph and E  C the set of exit states, core states
whose successor lies outside the core.

                                                            150
Definition A.18 (Exit-return map). Assume GC is acyclic. For each exit state e  E that
returns to the core (first re-entry state r(e)  C), follow the unique core path from r(e) to its
own exit (e)  E. The exit-return map H has vertex set E and edges e  (e) for each
returning exit.

Theorem A.19 (Exit-return equivalence). If GC is acyclic, then GC+ is acyclic if and only if
H is acyclic.

Proof. () If GC+ has a directed cycle , then  uses at least one return edge (since GC is
acyclic). Between consecutive return edges, the cycle follows core edges deterministically from
a re-entry to the next exit. Compressing each such segment to the edge e  (e) in H yields
a cycle in H.

    () A cycle e1  e2  · · ·  ek  e1 in H expands: for each i, concatenate the return
edge ei  r(ei) with the core path from r(ei) to (ei) = ei+1. This produces a directed cycle
in GC+.

Theorem A.20 (Composite ranking function). Assume GC is acyclic and H is acyclic. Let
L(e) denote the longest-path depth from e in H, d(x) the number of core edges from x to its
exit, and D = maxx d(x). Then the function

                                        V (x) = (D + 1) L e(x) + d(x)

satisfies V (y)  V (x) - 1 for every edge x  y in GC+.

Proof. For a core edge x  y: e(x) = e(y) and d(y) = d(x)-1, so V (y) = V (x)-1. For a return
edge e  r(e): L((e))  L(e) - 1 and d(r(e))  D, giving V (r(e))  (D+1)(L(e)-1) + D =
(D+1)L(e) - 1 = V (e) - 1.

Computational verification. Table 10 confirms the reduction in the exact (r, b) model. The
exit-return map H is roughly 60× smaller than G+M , and Theorem A.19 equivalence holds at
every M .

The theorems reduce Conjecture A.17 to two independent statements: (i) the core is acyclic,
and (ii) the exit-return map is acyclic. Statement (i) is the Layer 1 problem (carry parity +
no cycle birth). Statement (ii) concentrates Layer 2 into a boundary graph that is roughly
60× smaller than the full augmented graph, making it a more tractable target for future proof
efforts.

A.10 The s-invariant and positive-subgraph acyclicity

We now prove that the positive-drift subgraph of GM is always acyclic, for every M , via a
simple ranking function.

Definition A.21 (s-invariant). For an odd residue r, define s(r) = v2(r + 1), the 2-adic
valuation of r + 1.

                           151
Theorem A.22 (Positive-subgraph acyclicity). Let G+M denote the subgraph of GM restricted
to positive-drift edges (those with v2(3r + 1) = 1). Then s(r) = v2(r + 1) is a strict ranking
function on G+M : every positive step decreases s by exactly 1. Consequently GM + is acyclic, with

longest chain equal to M .

Proof. A positive step occurs when v2(3r + 1) = 1, i.e. r  1 (mod 4). Then T (r) = (3r + 1)/2

and                     3r +  1           3r + 3     3(r + 1)
                           2                 2           2.
     T (r) + 1    =              +  1  =          =

Since 3 is odd and r + 1  2 (mod 4) (because r  1 (mod 4)),

     v2 T (r) + 1 = v2(3) + v2(r + 1) - 1 = v2(r + 1) - 1 = s(r) - 1.

So s(T (r)) = s(r) - 1 on every positive step, making s a strict ranking. Since s  1 always, any
positive chain starting from s(r) = k has length at most k. The maximum occurs at r = 2M - 1

where s(r) = M , giving chains of length exactly M .

A.11 Reload dynamics and the budget equation

Theorem A.22 shows that purely positive chains are bounded. A net-positive cycle must there-
fore include negative steps (v2(3r + 1)  2) that "reload" the s-budget. We now analyze this
reload mechanism.

Definition A.23 (Reload). At a negative step with v2(3r + 1) = v  2, write q = (3r + 1)/2v
(which is odd). The reload at this step is v2(q + 1), i.e. the new s-value of the successor.

Proposition A.24 (Budget equation). Let  be a cycle of length n in GM with k positive steps

(each with v2 = 1) and n - k negative steps. Then the s-invariant returns to its starting value,

so

     k=                 s(ri+1) - s(ri) =         si (total reload).

     total drain  ineg                     ineg

Proof. Over a full cycle the s-value returns to its starting value. Each positive step decreases
s by exactly 1 (Theorem A.22), contributing -k total. The negative steps must compensate,
giving total reload = k.

Proposition A.25 (Geometric reload distribution under uniform modular lifts [Supporting]).
Among all v2 = 2 negative steps at level M , under the uniform distribution on residues mod 2M ,
the reload value v2(q + 1) follows an exact geometric distribution:

                                   Pr reload = j = 2-j, j = 1, 2, 3, . . .

Proof. For v2 = 2 steps: r  1 (mod 4), so q = (3r + 1)/4. Then q + 1 = (3r + 5)/4 =
(3(r + 1) + 2)/4. Since r + 1  2 (mod 4), write r + 1 = 2m with m ranging uniformly over
residues mod 2M-1. Then q + 1 = (6m + 2)/4 = (3m + 1)/2. Since m is equidistributed
mod 2M-1, the value 3m + 1 is equidistributed among even residues, and v2((3m + 1)/2) follows
the standard geometric law.

Remark A.26 (Approximate reload for general negative steps). Over all negative steps (any

v2  2), numerical evidence suggests the reload distribution is approximately Geometric(1/2).
Computational verification at M = 14, 16, 18 confirms the distribution matches 2-j to within

3% at each level. This approximation is not proved for the general case.

Remark A.27 (Reload rigidity). The M = 12 length-6 cycle sustains five consecutive positive
steps (draining s from 6 to 1) via a single reload of 5: an event with probability 2-5 = 3.1%
under the geometric distribution. As M grows, the cycle closure equation r · (3n - 2V )  -Cn
(mod 2M ) increasingly constrains which residues can participate, making such large reloads

harder to arrange. The reload rigidity conjecture asserts that for M sufficiently large, the

budget equation and cycle closure equation cannot be simultaneously satisfied for any net-

positive parameter pair (n, V ).

B Fiber-57 structural programme and the information bottle-
     neck

This section develops a complementary reduction of the Collatz conjecture through fine-grained
analysis of the drift-sustaining-chain mechanism at fiber 57 (the residue class n  57 (mod 64)
responsible for "drift-sustaining" continuations of long burst chains). The analysis produces
three main results: an information-theoretic memory demand (the pair-return cost c0  2.989 bits),
an absorption bottleneck limiting the supply channel to  log2 5  2.322 bits, and a branch
anti-concentration reduction showing that the sole remaining obstacle is orbitwise mixing of the
return-branch process.

B.1 Drift-sustaining chains and the pair-return automaton

A drift-sustaining chain at fiber 57 is a maximal run of consecutive burst-ending times at which
the quotient q = n/64 has its lowest base-8 digit in the sustaining set {0, 3, 7}. The chain
map q  9q + 8 (mod 8r) governs single-step continuation within a chain.

Proposition B.1 (Exact depth-2 known-gap partial return kernel). The depth-2 invariant core
I2 = {07, 33, 37, 73, 77}8 = {7, 27, 31, 59, 63} admits a 5 × 5 sub-stochastic partial return kernel
B~2 whose rows are indexed by I2 and whose (s, s)-entry is the probability that one I2-return
from s lands in the residue class s (mod 64). The kernel aggregates the two exactly resolved
return channels: the q  7 gap-2 block (Proposition B.3) and the q  3 gap-5 cylinder family
(Theorem B.5). It does not include q  3 returns with gap  6, which remain unresolved. The
Perron root of this known-gap partial kernel is exactly (B~2) = 129/1024  0.1260. The implied
per-return information cost is c0 = log2(1024/129)  2.989 bits.

Proof sketch. The q  7 branch returns in exactly two odd-to-odd steps with destination quo-
tient q = 9m + 8, uniform mod 8 (Proposition B.3). Because 9m + 8  0 (mod 8), the
destination quotient is  0 (mod 8), so its lowest octal digit is 0. Since 0 / {0, 3, 7}8  I2
(note: the element 07  I2 has octal representation 07, i.e. second digit 0, lowest digit 7), the

                                                            153
gap-2 channel contributes a zero row for s = 7 in the 5 × 5 kernel. For s  {27, 59} (octal
33, 73; lowest digit 3), the q  3 branch cannot return in fewer than 5 steps (Proposition B.4);
the gap-5 returns form an infinite family of dyadic cylinders indexed by w = v2(243m + 119)
(Theorem B.5), within each of which the destination is uniform mod 64 (odd slope 243). Each
s  {27, 59} contributes probability 1/2048 to each column of B~2. For s  {31, 63} (octal 37, 77;
lowest digit 7), the gap-2 channel gives destination q = 9m + 8 uniform mod 8; restricting to
q mod 64  I2 yields the nonzero entries: s = 31 maps to {27, 59} at probability 1/8 each, and
s = 63 maps to {7, 31, 63} at probability 1/8 each. The resulting 5 × 5 matrix B~2 has Perron
root (B~2) = 129/1024, verified by exact computation of its characteristic polynomial.

Remark B.2 (Three operators on the invariant core). Three distinct operators act on the
depth-r invariant core Ir and must not be conflated:

   1. B~r, the sub-stochastic partial return kernel, whose (s, s)-entry is the probability that
       one fiber-57 return from residue class s lands in class s while remaining inside Ir. Row
       sums are < 1 because many returns exit Ir. Known-gap Perron root: (B~2) = 129/1024
       (aggregating gap-2 and gap-5 channels only).

   2. P , the row-stochastic version obtained by normalizing each row of B~r to sum to 1. Perron
       root: 1 (by construction). P describes the conditional distribution of the next Ir-return
       given that one occurs.

   3. Pg, the gap-conditioned kernel, obtained by stratifying returns by their gap length g
       (number of fiber-57 visits between consecutive Ir-returns). Each Pg is row-stochastic; the
       mixture B~r = g g Pg recovers the sub-stochastic kernel, where g is the probability of
       gap length g.

B~r is the sub-stochastic kernel relevant to the known-gap information budget; P and Pg are
auxiliary normalized kernels used in the equidistribution and anti-concentration analysis. The
algebraic chain map, the sub-stochastic return kernel, and the normalized branch kernels are
distinct objects and should not be identified with one another.

B.2 Exact return structure of the sustaining branches

The depth-2 return kernel acts on the five elements of I2 = {7, 27, 31, 59, 63}, whose lowest octal
digits are 7, 3, 7, 3, 7 respectively. The two sustaining branches--q  3 and q  7 (mod 8)--
have distinct return structures. The following two propositions establish the exact first-return
structure for each branch.

Proposition B.3 (q  7 two-step return). Let S(n) = (3n + 1)/2v2(3n+1) be the odd-to-odd
Syracuse map, n = 64q + 57 with q  7 (mod 8), and write q = 8m + 7. Then:

   1. S(n) = 384m + 379  59 (mod 64) (not fiber 57);
   2. S2(n) = 576m + 569 = 64(9m + 8) + 57  57 (mod 64), so the orbit returns to fiber 57 in

       exactly two odd-to-odd steps;
   3. the returned quotient is q = 9m + 8, hence q  m (mod 8).
In particular, when m mod 8 is uniform, the destination q mod 8 is exactly uniform. The
chain map acts as a left shift in base 8: the lowest digit of q equals the second digit of q.
After r consecutive q  7 returns, all r low base-8 digits of the quotient have been replaced by
higher-order digits originally above the depth-r window.

Proof. Direct computation. 3n + 1 = 3(512m + 505) + 1 = 1536m + 1516 = 4(384m + 379).
Since 384m + 379 is odd, v2 = 2 and S(n) = 384m + 379  59 (mod 64). Next, 3S(n) + 1 =
1152m + 1138 = 2(576m + 569). Since 576m + 569 is odd, v2 = 1 and S2(n) = 576m + 569 =
64(9m + 8) + 57. The digit-shift property follows from q mod 8 = (9m + 8) mod 8 = m mod 8 =
(q - 7)/8 mod 8.

                                                            154
Proposition B.4 (q  3 minimum return gap). Let n = 64q + 57 with q  3 (mod 8), and
write q = 8m + 3, so n = 512m + 249. Then Sk(n)  57 (mod 64) for k = 1, 2, 3, 4. The

minimum fiber-57 return gap from the q  3 branch is  5 odd-to-odd steps.

Proof. Direct computation of each iterate:

             k Sk(n)                            v2 Sk(n) mod 64

             1 384m + 187 2                            59
             2 576m + 281 1                            25
             3 432m + 211 2                        48m + 19
             4 648m + 317 1                        8m + 61

Steps 1­2 give constant residues = 57. At step 3: 48m + 19  57 (mod 64) requires 48m  38
(mod 64); since gcd(48, 64) = 16  38, no solution exists. At step 4: 8m + 61  57 (mod 64)
requires 8m  60 (mod 64); since gcd(8, 64) = 8  60, no solution exists. The valuation path
for the first possible return (at step 5, conditional on m) is v = (2, 1, 2, 1, 3), injecting vi = 9
new low-order bits into the quotient.

Theorem B.5 (Gap-5 cylinder family). In the setting of Proposition B.4, the fourth iterate
is S4(n) = 648m + 317, so 3S4(n) + 1 = 8(243m + 119). Let w = v2(243m + 119). Then
S5(n) = (243m + 119)/2w, and S5(n) returns to fiber 57 if and only if

             243m + 119  57 · 2w (mod 2w+6).                        (59)

Because 243 is odd, it is invertible modulo every 2k, so for each w  0 there is a unique residue

class

             m  aw (mod 2w+6), aw = 243-1(57 · 2w - 119) mod 2w+6.

The cylinders {m  aw} are pairwise disjoint (indexed by w = v2(243m+119)), and their union

has density        1                        1          1       1
                 2w+6                       64     1 - 1/2     32
                       =                        ·           =

             w0

in m-space. Within each cylinder, the destination quotient takes the form q = k0(w) + 243t
where t parameterises the cylinder; since gcd(243, 64) = 1, the distribution of q mod 64 is

exactly uniform as t varies.

Proof. The identity S4(n) = 648m + 317 is verified by direct computation (Proposition B.4).
The condition S5(n)  57 (mod 64) becomes (59) by the definition of the odd-to-odd Syracuse
step. Invertibility of 243 modulo 2w+6 is immediate from gcd(243, 2) = 1. Disjointness follows

from the fact that v2(243m + 119) is a function of m. For the destination uniformity: writing
m = aw + 2w+6t gives (243m + 119)/2w = (243aw + 119)/2w + 243 · 26 · t = 57 + 64(k0 + 243t), so
q = k0 + 243t, and q mod 64 cycles through all 64 residues as t increases (since gcd(243, 64) =

1).

Remark B.6 (Kernel decomposition). Propositions B.3 and B.4, together with Theorem B.5,
decompose the fiber-57 return kernel into three channels: the q  7 branch (empirically 14%
of visits, 2-step return with uniform destination and zero retained memory), the q  3 gap-5
cylinders (3%, density 1/32 in m-space, destination uniform mod 64, slope 243), and all other
returns (gap  6 from q  3, or non-sustaining branches, 83%). The q  7 channel acts
as a regeneration event: the orbit's chain-membership information at depth r is erased after
r consecutive q  7 returns. The entire proof pressure for the orbitwise bound c < c0 therefore
concentrates on the q  3 complement.

                                            155
Remark B.7 (Invariant core rigidity). The algebraic chain map q  9q +8 (mod 8r) restricted
to the invariant core Ir (Proposition B.10 below) is a permutation at every depth r  2:
three fixed points and one 2-cycle, with HAB = 0 (no element of Ir ever maps outside Ir).
Consequently the spectral radius of the algebraic first-hit operator on Ir is 1, not 129/1024.
The sub-stochastic contraction of the known-gap partial kernel ((B~2) = 129/1024) arises from
the branching structure of the actual Collatz dynamics: the orbit does not always follow the
q  7 branch, and the probability of returning to Ir at each fiber-57 visit is strictly less than 1.

Proposition B.8 (Non-autonomy of fiber-57 returns). The fiber-57 first-return map on Ir is
not determined by q mod 8r alone: for the q  7 branch, the destination quotient q mod 8r
depends on the (r+1)-th base-8 digit of q. In particular, the algebraic chain map q  9q + 8
(mod 8r) and the actual first-return map are distinct operations.

Proof. For q  7 (mod 8), write q = 8m + 7. The two-step return (Proposition B.3) gives
destination quotient q = 9m + 8, so q mod 8r depends on m = (q - 7)/8, which reads the
base-8 digits of q at positions 1 through r (i.e., including position r, which lies above the depth-r
window q mod 8r). Concretely, at depth r = 2: the chain map sends 63  575  63 (mod 64)
(a fixed point), but the actual return gives q = 9 · 7 + 8 = 71  7 (mod 64) (a different
I2 element).

Remark B.9. Proposition B.8 explains why the permutation structure of the chain map on Ir
(Remark B.7) does not imply mixing: the actual dynamics inject higher-order digit information
at each return, and it is this digit-shift mechanism that causes orbits to escape Ir when higher
digits are generic (non-Cantor), even though the chain map keeps Ir invariant.

B.3 The invariant core and projective limit

Proposition B.10 (Bounded invariant core). For all r  2, let Cr := {q  Z/8rZ : all base-8 digits 
{0, 3, 7}} denote the depth-r Cantor set (|Cr| = 3r). The invariant core Ir  Cr of the chain map
q  9q + 8 (mod 8r) has exactly 5 elements: three fixed points q = k · 8r-1 - 1 for k  {1, 4, 8}
and one 2-cycle. The core density is 5/8r.

Theorem     B.11 (Projective  limit).  lim- Ir  = {-1} in the 8-adic integers.    The unique infinite
compatible  sequence is ar =  8r - 1.  Each     individual chain at fiber 57 has  length  log8(q +

1) + O(1).

Remark B.12 (From 8-adic to positive integers). If a positive integer n could sustain drift-
sustaining chains of unbounded length in fiber 57, its residue qr := n mod 8r would satisfy
qr  Ir for arbitrarily large r. By compatibility of the projection maps, the sequence (qr) would
define an element of lim- Ir = {-1}. Since -1 is not a positive integer, no positive n can achieve
this.

    More precisely, a positive integer n satisfies n mod 8r = 8r - 1 for all r > log8(n + 1), so it
is expelled from Ir at finite depth. This gives the chain-length bound above.

    Caveat. This argument shows that fixed drift-sustaining chains cannot persist, but it does

not by itself rule out an orbit that revisits fiber 57 via different chain mechanisms at different
depths. Ruling out such behavior is precisely the content of the open orbitwise bound c < c0.

B.4 The absorption theorem

Theorem B.13 (Mod-8r absorption, verified for r  10). For r = 2, 3, . . . , 10 (verified by
exhaustive computation), for every s  Ir, the Collatz orbit starting from n0 = 64(s + 8r · L) + 57
(with L  1 so that n0 has digits above position r) reaches n  1 (mod 8r) within O(r) steps,
without the quotient n/64 mod 8r revisiting any element of Ir along the way. Maximum
absorption time:  194 steps. All 5 elements absorb to the same fixed point 1 (mod 8r).

                                                156
Proof (computational). Exhaustive integer arithmetic for each of the 5 elements of Ir at each
depth r = 2, . . . , 10, using starting values n = 64(s + 8r · 10,000) + 57 to produce genuinely large
inputs. All 45 orbits (5 × 9) reach 1 (mod 8r) within 194 steps without revisiting Ir.

    Note on small quotients. For the bare element q = s (without offset L), an immediate
Ir-return can occur because no higher digits are available to break the chain-map alignment.
For example, q = 63  I2 returns in two steps to q = 71  7 (mod 64)  I2, because the second
base-8 digit of q = 63 = 778 is itself 7. The large-offset construction L  1 ensures that higher
digits provide the generic disruption that drives absorption.

Remark B.14 (Scope and generalization). The theorem is established by exhaustive computa-
tion for r  10. The pattern is expected to persist for all r  2 by the projective limit argument
(Theorem B.11): since lim- Ir = {-1} and -1 is not a positive integer, the core elements at
each depth r are "eventually incompatible" with any fixed positive orbit. A general proof for all
r would require showing that the absorption trajectories remain compatible across the 8-adic
tower, which we leave open.

B.5 The Absorption Bottleneck Lemma

Lemma B.15 (Absorption Bottleneck). Assume absorption holds at depth r. Then the inter-
chain information channel has capacity Cchannel  log2 |Ir| = log2 5  2.322 bits. The known-gap
depth-2 per-return demand is c(02,known) = log2(1024/129)  2.989 bits (from Proposition B.1),
giving a deficit per return of c0(2,known) - log2 5  0.667 bits. (Including the unresolved gap- 6
tail would decrease c0, reducing this margin.)

Proof. By the absorption theorem, every element of Ir follows a deterministic trajectory to fixed
point 1 (mod 8r). The post-absorption state is a function f : Ir  S with |f (Ir)|  |Ir| = 5,
giving H(f (q))  log2 5. The critical inequality is 5 < 1024/129, equivalently 645 < 1024.

Remark B.16 (M-value collapse). Computationally, the actual channel capacity is much less
than log2 5. For r  3, all 5 elements of Ir typically produce the same post-absorption state
(capacity = 0), because the absorption paths merge through common residues {-7, -5, 5, 1}
(mod 8r) before reaching the fixed point.

Corollary B.17 (Geometric decay from the known-gap budget). Under the known-gap depth-2
kernel, consecutive inter-chain Ir-returns are bounded by P (K consecutive)  K with  =
645/1024  0.630. (Including the unresolved gap- 6 contributions would increase .) Empir-
ically, at r = 2, 97% of inter-chain streaks have length 1; for r  3, zero consecutive Ir  Ir
transitions were observed.

B.6 The memory lower bound and theorem ladder

Theorem B.18 (Memory lower bound). Under the known-gap depth-2 demand, r pair returns
to fiber 57 require Mreq(r)  r · log2(1024/129) - O(1)  2.989 r bits of new information.

    The theorem ladder has three rungs:
 (A) Fixed-k impossibility (proved): a causal k-bit controller cannot sustain core alignment

       beyond depth rmax = k/3 + O(1).
 (B) Memory lower bound (proved): Theorem B.18.
 (C) Orbit memory upper bound (open): c < c0. Numerical evidence suggests c  0.51,

       substantially below c0, but this remains unproved.

                                                            157
B.7 Branch anti-concentration reduction

Proposition B.19 (Branchwise permutation). In each drift-sustaining-continuation branch,
the quotient update m  9m + c (mod Mf ) with gcd(9, Mf ) = 1 is a permutation on Z/Mf Z.
No single residue can be amplified by the affine step.
Corollary B.20 (Concentration source). Any max-atom concentration above the threshold
1/2 (fiber 57) or 1/3 (fiber 29) must arise from orbitwise concentration on a subset of return
branches, not from the quotient algebra.

    This yields the target lemma for closure:
Remark B.21 (Branch anti-concentration target). A proof that the return-branch process at
fiber 57 cannot place more than 1/2 of its mass on any mod-8 residue class would close the
framework. Equivalently, showing that the branch-memory coefficient g  0 as the inter-
return gap g   suffices, since long gaps occur with overwhelming frequency (Ir density
= 5/8r).

B.8 Carry equidistribution and empirical verification

Proposition B.22 (Inter-chain equidistribution (empirical)). Among fiber-57 visits separated
by more than 5 Collatz steps (inter-chain transitions), the Ir-return rate is at or below baseline:
0.70× at r = 2, 0.31× at r = 3, 0.34× at r = 4. The worst-case normalised ratio R2  0.70
(i.e., visits to I2 occur at 70% of baseline density) gives c = - log2(0.70)  0.51 bits, far below
c(02,known) = 2.989. Verified over 5,000 orbits, confirmed by random-start controls.
Remark B.23 (Gap-induced branch decorrelation). Full 8 × 8 transition matrices for the mod-
8 quotient class were measured at various gap lengths. At gaps > 50, the 2 independence
test yields p = 0.18 (no significant correlation), confirming that branch memory decays with
gap length. At short gaps ( 5), significant correlation persists (p < 10-10), as expected from
intra-chain structure.

B.9 Path-conditional bijection and annealed closure

Theorem B.24 (Path-conditional bijection). For every valuation path  = (v1, . . . , vk) with
V = vi and V  3, the fresh-digit map t  (3kt + d) mod q is a bijection on Z/qZ with
coefficient 3k, since gcd(3k, q) = 1 for q = 8 or 16. The coefficient identity 29-V · 2V -3/64 = 1
ensures the map is well-defined.

    This gives 57  1/8 < 1/2 and 29  1/16 < 1/3, closing the annealed anti-concentration
completely, while leaving the deterministic orbitwise upgrade (Conjecture 12.2) open.

B.10 Summary: the quantitative reduction

The fiber-57 programme produces a complementary reduction of the Collatz conjecture, inde-
pendent of (and consistent with) the WMH-based reduction in Sections 4­9:

C Unconditional cylinder-averaged closure on I2 via spectral
     contraction

This section presents an unconditional strengthening of the fiber-57 programme that operates
entirely at the cylinder-averaged level (the natural setting of the uniform-fiber lemma, Propo-
sition 9.112). The main outcome is a fully rigorous contraction source on the invariant core
I2 that does not invoke the spectator-bit mechanism, and therefore provides an independent,
non-circular route to the orbit-level total-variation summability of Corollary 9.119.

    The section is organized around four results whose combined effect is to replace the single
genuinely circular step identified in Remark 9.139 by a finite, explicit, unconditional computa-
tion:

   1. a Ruffini reduction showing that the depth-2 known-gap return kernel B~2 has maximally
       degenerate spectrum, with finite-time rank-1 collapse (§C.1);

   2. an unconditional bound (B~2ext)  5/32 on the full extended kernel, valid without any
       distributional assumption (§C.2);

   3. an exponential tail on the time to enter I2, derived from the 27 × 27 cylinder-averaged
       mod-64 kernel (§C.3);

   4. an exact stationary mass (I2) = 10121/65280  0.15504, giving an unconditional orbit-
       level sum bound c Pr(ER(c))  0.011 at the cylinder-averaged level (§C.4).

The combined claim is a cylinder-averaged unconditional TV summability, which upgrades
Theorem 9.144 to an unconditional density-1 convergence statement without relying on the
spectator-bit loop. We do not claim a pointwise orbit-level result: the bridge from cylinder-
averaged to pointwise convergence continues to go through the Borel­Cantelli argument of
Theorem 9.144.

C.1 Ruffini reduction of the depth-2 known-gap kernel

Recall from Proposition B.1 the depth-2 known-gap partial return kernel B~2 on the invariant
core I2 = {7, 27, 31, 59, 63}, a 5 × 5 sub-stochastic matrix with entries in Q.

                                159
Proposition C.1 (Ruffini collapse of B~2). The characteristic polynomial of B~2 factors as

                                  B~2 ()       =      4     -  129   ,
                                                               1024

so the Perron root is (B~2) = 129/1024 with algebraic multiplicity 1, and  = 0 has algebraic
multiplicity 4. Consequently rank(B~2k) = 1 for all k  2.

Proof. Direct symbolic computation using Ruffini's synthetic-division rule on the 5 × 5 rational
matrix from Proposition B.1. The trace identity tr(B~2k) = (129/1024)k for k = 1, 2, 3 confirms
the factorization and pins down the nilpotent part. Finite-time rank collapse to 1 follows from
the Jordan form: the nilpotent block has size at most 4 but vanishes by the second power since
the non-zero singular structure is concentrated on the single row corresponding to the q  7
residue class.

Remark C.2 (Interpretation). Proposition C.1 says that the known-gap component of the
depth-2 return dynamics is already spectrally exhausted: no refinement of the known-gap kernel
can produce a better contraction rate than 129/1024  0.126. In particular, the dynamical
obstruction to a full convergence proof does not live inside B~2; it lives in the unresolved gap-
 6 channels that are absent from Proposition B.1. The natural next step is therefore to enlarge
B~2 to include these channels and ask whether the bound survives an uncontrolled forcing.

C.2 The unconditional extended spectral bound

Let B~2ext denote the full return kernel obtained from B~2 by adjoining the contribution of all gap-
g  6 channels, weighted by their cylinder masses. Under uniform forcing (the cylinder-averaged
kernel), B~2ext is the exact return operator on I2, not merely the known-gap approximation.

Theorem C.3 (Unconditional spectral bound on I2). The cylinder-averaged extended depth-2

return kernel satisfies                                    5
                                                       32 ,
                                              B~2ext

with equality attained at the critical forcing level  = 155/2048. Consequently the per-return

information cost on I2 satisfies

                                  c(02,ext)    log2   32    2.678 bits.
                                                      5

The bound is unconditional: its proof uses only the odd-slope uniform-fiber lemma (Proposi-
tion 9.112) and the disjointness of the gap-cylinders of Theorem B.5.

Proof. The extended kernel decomposes as B~2ext = B~2 + F , where F is a non-negative matrix
supported on the gap- 6 cylinders. Under the uniform-fiber cylinder averaging, each column

of F is uniform across I2 by Proposition 9.112 (the image of any cylinder of length  6 is an
equidistributed fiber in Z/64Z). A direct symbolic calculation (see the reference implementa-
tion) then yields the closed form (B~2 +·U ) = 129/1024+2/5, where U is the column-uniform
forcing matrix and  the total forcing mass. The maximum admissible forcing mass, obtained

from cylinder disjointness via g6 g  31/32 (the complement of the gap-5 mass 5 = 1/32),
is  = 155/2048. Substituting gives (B~2ext)  129/1024 + 2 · (155/2048)/5 = 5/32. The
information-cost bound follows by c0 = log2(1/).

Remark C.4 (Why this bound is unconditional). Theorem C.3 does not invoke the spectator-

bit mechanism of Remark 9.117. The distributional input it requires is that the cylinder dis-
tribution within I2 is uniform -- which follows from the algebraic fact gcd(3k, 64) = 1, i.e. the
odd-slope unit property in Proposition 9.112. It does not require the cascade-exit iterate to be
uniform modulo 2S+R, nor does it require Pr(ER) to be small a priori. The spectral contrac-
tion is therefore an independent source of cycle-level decay that runs parallel to -- rather than

through -- the loop identified in Remark 9.139.

                                                  160
C.3 Transient-prefix exponential tail on the cylinder-averaged mod-64 chain

To quantify the time an orbit spends outside I2 before first entering the core, we compute the
Perron root of the transient block of the exact cylinder-averaged mod-64 kernel.

Definition C.5 (Cylinder-averaged mod-64 kernel). For each odd residue r  {1, 3, . . . , 63} and
each lift n = r + 64k with 0  k < 27, apply one Collatz odd step n  (3n + 1)/2v2(3n+1) and
record the successor's residue modulo 64. Averaging over the 27 lifts with the uniform weight
yields a rational 32 × 32 stochastic matrix P , the cylinder-averaged mod-64 kernel.

    The averaging over lifts mod 213 is the uniform measure on cylinders of length up to 12 (since
v2(3n + 1)  7 for n < 213), and is an exact consequence of Proposition 9.112; no distributional
assumption about orbits is used.

Theorem C.6 (Transient-prefix exponential tail). Let Q be the 27 × 27 submatrix of P ob-
tained by deleting the five rows and columns indexed by I2 = {7, 27, 31, 59, 63} (marking I2 as
absorbing). Then the Perron root of Q is

      (Q) = 0.8632924 . . . ,

and the first hitting time TI2 satisfies the unconditional exponential tail

Pr(TI2 > N )  27 · (Q) N  27 · 2-0.2121 N ,                       N  0,

with expected hitting time at most 10.142 steps from the worst transient starting state, and
half-life - log 2/ log (Q)  4.72 Collatz steps.

Proof. The matrix Q is non-negative, sub-stochastic, and irreducible on the 27 transient states

(direct inspection). The Perron­Frobenius theorem gives a unique dominant eigenvalue, which

is computed numerically via eigendecomposition of the exact rational matrix, verified by the
power iteration Qk  (Q)k to ten decimal places. The tail bound follows by Pr(TI2 > N |
start = s) = (QN 1)s  |QN 1|  QN   27 (Q)N (the crude factor 27 is the -to-1
inclusion on the transient block, and can be sharpened by a factor of 2 via the left Perron

eigenvector, which is numerically close to uniform on 20 of the 27 transient states). Expected
hitting time is the row sum of the fundamental matrix (I - Q)-11.

Remark C.11 (Comparison with the prior conditional figure). The bound of Corollary C.10 is

The Cramér exponent and slope agree to all six computed decimal places. The MGF prefac-
tor CCher scales linearly with |T | (hence ×8 from the ×8 lift), but this prefactor cancels in

tively from any k  6 down to actual integer Collatz cycles, so the maximum cycle mean is
invariant. The mod-64 chain therefore already saturates the spectral information available from
the cylinder-averaged framework, and refining to mod 2k for any k > 6 produces no sharper
exponent. Verification scripts: mod512.py, mod512_v2.py.

Remark C.16 (Why the graph version fails). A purely graph-theoretic version of Theorem C.12
-- "no closed walk in T has positive average per-edge log-growth" -- is false at the cylinder-
averaged resolution. Karp's maximum cycle mean algorithm applied to the per-edge log-growth
weights of Q gives

µcycle  =    max      T     1          (log2 3 - v)    +0.0850 bits/edge,
                         |cycle|
           cycles in              edges

so positive-drift cycles exist inside T . The honest no-escape statement is therefore distributional,
not deterministic, and is precisely Theorem C.12. The expectation-level bound is strictly weaker
as a graph statement but strictly sufficient for the orbit-TV summability, because Vmax  0.88
bits is already smaller than the contraction log2(32/5)  2.678 bits earned by a single visit to
I2.

Lemma C.17 (Periodic exclusion on positive-mean cycles). Let C = (r0  r1  · · ·  rL-1 

r0) be any cycle in the transient digraph T on which the per-edge log-growth has positive average,
i.e. iL=-01(log2 3 - vi) > 0 (equivalently 3L > 2vi). Then no positive integer n admits a Collatz
prefix that is exactly periodic on C.

Proof. Iterating the accelerated odd map L times along C realises an affine map n  (3Ln +
R)/2vi for a fixed integer R depending only on the v-pattern. A periodic integer is a fixed
point of this map, requiring (3L - 2vi) n = -R. Since 3L > 2vi the affine multiplier strictly

exceeds 1, so the fixed point (if it existed) would be expanding and no positive integer can sit
on it; equivalently gcd(3, 2) = 1 forbids the divisibility relation 2vi | 3Ln + R being preserved

under iteration. Hence no integer is exactly periodic on C.

                                  164
Remark C.18 (Karp cycle of the mod-64 chain). The unique (up to rotation) maximal-mean

cycle of the 27-state transient digraph is 15  55  19  61  15 (mod 64), with edge v2-
pattern [1, 1, 1, 3], length L = 4, v = 6, per-cycle log-growth log2(81/64)  +0.3399 bits, and
mean +0.0850 bits/edge = µmax. By Lemma C.17, no integer is exactly periodic on this cycle.
Verification script: karp_cycle.py.

Theorem C.19 (Cycle-tracking polynomial envelope). Let (n) := log2 n - log2 n denote the
prefix log-growth of an integer n before its Collatz trajectory enters I2 (mod 64). There exist

absolute constants C0, C1 > 0 such that, for every odd n  1,

(n)    log2(81/64)  log2 n  +  C0  =  0.0566 log2 n  +  C0,    n  C1 · n1.0566.
            v

where log2(81/64) = 0.3399 bits is the Karp per-lap log-growth (Remark C.18) and v = 6 is the
total 2-adic valuation absorbed along one full traversal of the optimal cycle. Equivalently, writing
µmax = 0.0850 for the per-edge Karp mean and lap := 2v = 64 for the per-lap congruence-

multiplicity factor, the slope equals µmax · L/ log2 lap = 0.0850 · 4/6 = 0.0566.

Sketch. By Lemma C.17 no integer is exactly periodic on any positive-mean cycle. To track

the optimal cycle of Remark C.18 for k consecutive laps, an integer must satisfy, at each lap, a
fresh 2-adic constraint of size 2v = 26: the product (3n + 1)/2v1 must land in the next residue

of the cycle and reveal exactly vi+1 trailing zeros on the following step. These constraints are
independent across laps, so the smallest k-lap follower scales as n  klap = 64k; equivalently,
the longest cycle prefix realised by any n  [1, 2N ] is at most N/ log2 lap + O(1) = N/6 + O(1)
laps. Each lap contributes log2(81/64) = 0.3399 bits of log-growth, and off-cycle excursions are
absorbed by the spectral contraction (Q) < 1 already established for Q on T . Summing the

on-cycle and off-cycle contributions gives the stated linear bound on (n).
    Empirical verification (direct_verify_c.c) over [1, 232] (roughly 2· 109 odd integers,  75 s

wall-clock) records a strict staircase of record holders:

       k (laps)                 smallest n    ratio       (n)  log2 n
               0                         111     --  +1.1771     6.79
               1                                     +1.5170
               2                      6,863   61.8   +1.8569   12.74

The ratio of successive record holders has geometric mean 64.7, matching the theoretical lap =
2v = 64 to within 1%. Each additional lap increases  by exactly log2(81/64) = 0.3399 bits.
The empirical slope at n = 1,926,419,151 is / log2 n = 2.5293/30.84 = 0.0820, slightly above the
asymptotic slope 0.0566 because of the C0 tail contribution ( 0.78 bits of off-cycle entry/exit

excursion). As N grows the slope converges to the analytic bound 0.0566 from above.

Remark C.20 (Polynomial envelope is enough for orbit TV). Although Theorem C.19 is weaker
than a uniform constant pointwise bound, it is comfortably sufficient for orbit-TV summability:
the Chernoff exponent use  16.75 of Theorem C.13 dominates the polynomial envelope expo-
nent 0.0566 by a factor of nearly 300, leaving the density-(1 - n-15) closure of Corollary C.14
unaffected.

Theorem C.21 (Infinite BF staircase: cycle-tracking envelope is tight). For each integer k 
1 define the BF k-champion nk to be the smallest positive integer n such that the first 4k
accelerated odd-step iterates of n realise the residue pattern [15, 55, 19, 61]k (mod 64) with v2-
pattern [1, 1, 1, 3]k, and the (4k + 1)-th iterate has residue 47 (mod 64). The sequence {nk}
exists, is uniquely defined, and satisfies:

  (i) each nk is constructible by an explicit 2-adic Hensel-style lift, with the constraint set
       carving out a unique residue class modulo 26k+8;

                                      165
(ii) nk+1/nk  2v = 64 as k  , with geometric mean of the first four ratios exactly 64.0;

(iii) the prefix log-growth satisfies (nk) = k ·log2(81/64)+Cexit +o(1) = 0.3399 k +1.170+o(1),
      where Cexit = 1.170 bits is the exit-tail contribution from the two-step path 47  39  59;

(iv) consequently the asymptotic slope (nk)/ log2 nk converges to log2(81/64)/v = 0.0566 . . .
      as k  , matching exactly the analytic exponent of Theorem C.19.

The first eight values, computed by the lift (lift_bf.py), are:

k  nk log2 nk  (nk) ratio

1  6,863 12.74 +1.5170 --

2  316,111 18.27 +1.8569 46.1

3  22,205,135 24.40 +2.1968 70.2

4  1,926,419,151 30.84 +2.5293 86.8

5  115,206,181,583 36.75 +2.8692 59.8

6  5,303,526,675,151 42.27 +3.2090 46.0

7 372,540,410,352,335 48.40 +3.5489 70.2

8 32,319,950,267,011,791 54.84 +3.8887 86.8

The geometric mean of consecutive ratios over k = 4  8 is exactly 64 (i.e. n8/n4 = 16777216 =
224 = 644 to all computed digits), confirming the per-lap congruence-multiplicity factor analyti-
cally and numerically.

Sketch. The pattern in (i) is a Hensel-style lift problem: each constraint `(v2 at iterate i equals
vitgt, residue at iterate i + 1 equals rit+gt1)' tightens the residue class of n0 by vitgt bits. Summing
the bit-tightening over the full k-lap pattern [1, 1, 1, 3]k plus the two-step exit gives 6k + 8
bits of constraint, hence a unique residue class mod 26k+8 (verified empirically for k = 1, . . . , 8
where the lift returns exactly two solutions, related by the additive lattice structure of the tail
constraint, of which we take the smaller). Items (ii)­(iii) follow because each new lap multiplies
nk by 2v = 64 (mod the lower-order corrections from the exit tail) and adds exactly log2(81/64)
bits to . Item (iv) is then immediate from log2 nk = 6k +O(1) and (nk) = 0.3399 k +O(1).

Corollary C.22 (Pointwise envelope is logarithmic, not constant). supn (n) = +. In par-
ticular, no absolute constant C exists such that (n)  C for all n. The polynomial envelope of
Theorem C.19 is therefore tight at the asymptotic slope 0.0566 and cannot be replaced by any
sub-logarithmic bound.

Remark C.23 (Density of the BF staircase). The BF k-champion sequence has density zero
in N: |{nk : nk  N }| = log64 N + O(1) = O(log N ). Each nk lies in a residue class of size 1
modulo 26k+8, so the natural density of integers tracking the cycle for  k laps is  2-6k-8. The
BF staircase therefore has natural density 0, consistent with Corollary C.14's density-(1 - n-15)
closure. The staircase shows that the exceptional set in Corollary C.14 is genuinely non-empty
at every scale, but it is sparse and structured: it consists exactly of arithmetic progressions
whose moduli grow geometrically with the cycle-tracking depth.

C.5 The (C-2adic) bound on BF residue classes

The BF staircase identifies, for each k  1, a unique residue class

                                   Bk := { n  N : n  nk (mod 26k+8) }

that follows the cycle-conformal Karp pattern for k laps before escaping at residue 47 and
entering I2. The Hensel lift of Theorem C.21(i) shows that Bk is the exact fibre of nk under
the truncation Z2  Z/26k+8. We now state the auxiliary-memory bound this fibre supports.

   166
Theorem C.24 (Haar (C-2adic) bound). Let T2 : Z2  Z2 denote the Collatz 2-adic extension

of Lagarias [10], which is measure-preserving and ergodic with respect to Haar measure µ. Let
Bk  Z2 be the BF k-class above, viewed as a clopen ball of µ-measure 2-(6k+8). Then for
µ-almost every n  Bk the orbit T2j(n) is well defined, halts in finitely many odd steps if it hits
N, and the stopping time  (n) admits a uniform constant C  [0, ) such that

 (n)  C k + log2 n                 for µ-almost every n  Bk  N,

with C independent of k.

Sketch. By Lagarias [10], T2 is measure-preserving and ergodic, and the conjugation  : Z2  Z2
identifies the T2-action with the Bernoulli shift on {0, 1}N. Under this conjugation, the clopen
ball Bk pulls back to a cylinder of length 6k + 8 in {0, 1}N. The expected log-growth per odd
step in the shift coordinates equals log2 3 - 2 = -0.415 bits, strictly negative, so the ergodic
theorem applied to the additive cocycle (n) = log2(T2(n)/n) gives

1  N -1                   T2j (n)  -N---   dµ = log2 3 - 2 < 0
N
        

   j=0

for µ-a.e. n. The Birkhoff theorem furnishes a Haar-null exceptional set on Z2; restricting

to Bk  N and converting the linear log-decay to a hitting-time bound gives  (n)  C (k +
log2 n) with C depending only on the cocycle's L2 norm and the cylinder length offset 6k + 8.
Independence of k comes from the fact that the per-step drift is intrinsic to the shift, not to the

cylinder prefix.

Corollary C.25 (Empirical (C-2adic), 39 140-witness form). For each of 39 140 uniformly ran-
dom samples n  Bk drawn at 1  k  14 and 20  log2 n  171, the trajectory halts at 1 and
the bound  (n)  C (k + log2 n) holds with

C  5.04 for all 14 400 samples with k  9,
C  6.49 for all 15 000 samples with k  4 and log2 n  106,
C  9.87 unconditionally over the 39 140-sample record (single k = 2 small-denominator outlier).

The empirical mean of C is 2.78 ± 0.05, essentially flat in k and in log2 n, and the Crandall-
normalised stopping time  / log2 n averages  3.0, well below the heuristic prediction 2/ log(4/3) 
6.95. The samples are produced by the scripts c2adic_haar.py, c2adic_sweep_big.py, c2adic_tight_bigk.p
and c2adic_mega.py; the lift of nk to k = 14 is performed by champs_extend.py.

Proof. Direct enumeration and verification. For each sampled n the trajectory T2j(n) is com-
puted in exact arithmetic until it reaches 1, the stopping time  is recorded, and the ratio
C =  /(k + log2 n) is tabulated. The maxima reported follow from the order statistics of the re-
sulting empirical distributions; the sample sizes are 1 440+6 300+2 000+15 000+14 400 = 39 140,
partitioned across Push 366 (Haar test), Push 371­380 (extended sweep + tight k  8), and
Push 381­390 (mega sweep 9  k  14).

Remark C.26 (Residual obstruction: Haar / pointwise gap). Theorem C.24 delivers the (C-
2adic) bound on a Haar-conull subset of Bk. Because N is itself Haar-null inside Z2, the Haar
statement does not directly entail a pointwise statement on Bk  N: an a-priori-conceivable
obstruction is a Haar-null but N-supported failure stratum. This is the same Haar/pointwise gap
that obstructs the classical probabilistic Collatz arguments of Lagarias and Tao [14], transposed
to the BF cylinder. Three observations narrow it considerably:

(a) Corollary C.25 furnishes 39 140 explicit deterministic witnesses, all of which satisfy the
     bound with C  5.04 for k  9. Any failure stratum must therefore avoid every one of
     these uniform draws across 13 levels of k and 7 orders of magnitude in log2 n.

                                   167
  (b) The empirical Cmean is essentially flat in both k and log2 n, indicating that the bound's
       constant is scale-invariant and not in the process of degrading.

  (c) The Crandall-normalised mean  / log2 n  3.0 at k = 14 is below the generic Collatz
       heuristic, so the BF cylinders are not pathologically slow -- they halt faster than typical
       n. Any putative failure stratum would have to single out members of Bk that are much
       slower than their immediate 2-adic neighbours, while remaining missed by all N = 14 400
       uniform draws at k  9.

The residual obstruction is therefore confined to a Haar-null, sample-empty subset of N  Bk
at each k. Closing it deterministically is the same difficulty as the Haar/pointwise gap in Tao's
2019 result, and is left as the principal open problem of the (C-2adic) layer.

Remark C.27 (Scope and limitations). Three scope points are important to state plainly.
    (1) Cylinder-averaged, not pointwise. All four results (Propositions C.1, Theorems C.3, C.6,

C.7) are statements about the cylinder-averaged kernel P . A specific orbit may deviate from the
average over finitely many cycles. The bridge from cylinder-averaged to pointwise convergence
continues to go through the Borel­Cantelli argument of Theorem 9.144, which upgrades the
summability to density-1 orbit convergence. This section does not claim every orbit converges
-- it claims that the density-1 convergence is now unconditional.

    (2a) Distributional no-escape, not deterministic. Theorem C.12 bounds the expected log-
growth and expected hitting time, not the realized values for individual orbits. As Remark C.16
shows, the graph-theoretic "no positive-drift cycle" version is false; the honest statement is in
expectation.

    (2b) Non-divergence threshold is separate. The verification that no orbit with B0 = log2 n0 
68 can escape to infinity before reaching the threshold at which the spectral contraction engages
is a separate matter, handled in the existing paper by the Barina computational verification
and the 43-bit safety margin of Remark 9.137. Nothing in this section replaces or strengthens
that argument.

    (3) Independent of the WMH route. The WMH-based reduction of Sections 4­9 is not
affected or superseded. The present section provides a parallel, independent route to density-1
convergence through the fiber-57 / I2 programme, which the previous section summarized as
"complementary to the WMH-based reduction".

Remark C.28 (Update to Remark 9.139). Theorem C.3 provides a cycle-level contraction
source that is outside the loop identified in Remark 9.139. The chain

spectator bits  fiber mixing  IID model  exponential tail  TV summability  spectator bits

is broken at the single step "exponential tail  TV summability", because the exponential
tail of Theorem C.6 is derived from the finite-state 27 × 27 mod-64 chain rather than from
Proposition 9.118's IID-dependent derivation. The "irreducible hard core" identified at the end
of Remark 9.139 is still irreducible if one insists on a pointwise proof through the spectator-bit
mechanism; what this section shows is that the density-1 form of convergence is reachable by
bypassing that mechanism entirely.

C.6 Complete cascade algebra and the generic dynamics gap

The BF staircase of Theorem C.21 and the (C-2adic) bound of Theorem C.24 establish the
structural framework for orbits entering Bk. We now close the algebraic side completely: every
quantity in the BF cascade admits a closed-form formula, the carry chain governing generic
dynamics has a clean structural theorem, and the renewal drift is computed exactly. These
results sharpen the characterisation of the remaining gap.

                                                            168
Theorem C.29 (Complete cascade theorem). Let n  Bk for k  1. The deterministic BF
cascade consists of exactly k consecutive BF cycles [1, 1, 1, 3] with zero intervening generic steps:
every BF exit immediately enters the next BF cycle. The total deterministic region, in which
the valuation sequence is identical for all members of Bk, extends to exactly 4k + ek accelerated
odd steps, where ek = 6 for k  1, 2 (mod 4) and ek = 5 for k  0, 3 (mod 4). This comprises:

  (a) 4k steps of the BF phase (k laps of [1, 1, 1, 3], total growth k · log2(81/64) bits);

  (b) 4 steps of the exit tail [1, 1, 1, 2] (as in Proposition C.54(ii));

(c) ek - 4  {1, 2} additional deterministic steps beyond the exit tail, with the period-4 pattern
     determined by the auxiliary coefficient ak  {57, 41, 45, 61}.

The net gain after the full deterministic region is exactly log2 3 for k  0, 3 (mod 4) and
2 log2 3 - 1 for k  1, 2 (mod 4), both bounded constants independent of k.

Sketch. The zero-gap claim follows from the exit map exit(n) = (81n + 65)/64: for n  Bk with
k  2, one verifies exit(n)  Bk-1 by checking the Hensel residue mod 26(k-1)+8. Parts (a)

and (b) are Proposition C.54(i)­(ii). For part (c), the post-cascade value Pk has residue 47 mod

64, and the v-sequence from Pk begins [1, 1, 1, 2, . . .] (the exit tail). Beyond those 4 steps, the fifth
post-cascade step has v2 = 1 deterministically for all q (since v2(Pk +q·81k ·256) = 3+8 = 11 bits

of agreement), and a sixth step is deterministic for k  1, 2 (mod 4) (verified for k = 1, . . . , 12,
q = 0, . . . , 99). The period-4 pattern in ek follows from the period-4 cycling of ak in 17nk + 65 =
ak · 26k+5. The net gain formulas follow by exact computation of log2(T 4k+ek (nk)/nk).

Proposition C.30 (Post-cascade closed form). Define the post-cascade value Pk := exitk(nk)
as the value obtained after applying the BF exit map k times to nk. Then

                        Pk  =  ak  ·  81k · 32  -  65  ,
                                          17

where ak  {57, 41, 45, 61} cycles with period 4. Furthermore, Pk  47 (mod 64) for all k, and
for general n  Bk written as n = nk + m · 26k+8, the post-cascade value is Pk + m · 81k · 256.

The post-cascade values satisfy the recurrence

Pk+4  =  814  ·  Pk  +  65(814 -   1)  =  43046721        ·  Pk  +  164590400.
                             17

Proof. The iterated exit map satisfies exitk(n) = (81kn+65(81k -64k)/17)/64k, verified exactly
for k = 1, . . . , 7. Substituting n = nk and using 17nk + 65 = ak · 26k+5 gives the formula after
simplification. The congruence Pk  47 (mod 64) is checked for k = 1, . . . , 12. The recurrence

follows from ak+4 = ak and direct algebraic manipulation.

Remark C.31 (Notation reconciliation). The BF cascade involves three stages, each with its
own affine parameterization. For n = rk + 26k+8 q  Bk:

  (a) BF phase (4k steps): T 4k(n) = Ak(bf) + 81k · 256 q (Proposition C.54(i)).

  (b) Full exit (4k + 4 steps): T 4k+4(n) = Ak + Uk q with Uk = 34(k+1) · 8 = 81k+1 · 8 (Proposi-
       tion C.54(iii)).

  (c) Post-cascade (after k applications of exit): exitk(n) = Pk +m·81k ·256 (Proposition C.30).

Stages (a) and (c) use the same progression step 81k ·256, because exitk is equivalent to applying
the four-step BF map k times starting from n, so the free-parameter coefficient is (81/64)k ·
26k+8 = 81k · 256. Stage (b) adds one more four-step block (the exit tail [1, 1, 1, 2]), multiplying
the coefficient by 81/32 to get Uk = 81k+1·8. The relationship Uk = 81·81k ·8 = (81/32)·81k ·256
reconciles the two.

                                   169
Theorem C.32 (Carry chain structural theorem). For any odd n > 1 in the accelerated Collatz
map T (n) = odd(3n+1), define the v = 1 streak length L as the maximal number of consecutive
iterates with v2(3ni + 1) = 1. Then:

  (i) L = trailing_ones(n) - 1 exactly, where trailing_ones counts the number of consecutive
       1-bits at the least-significant end of n.

  (ii) After every v = 1 streak of length L  1, the recovery value nL = 2 · 3L · m - 1 (for some
       integer m) satisfies trailing_ones(nL) = 1, forcing v2(3nL + 1)  2.

 (iii) Conditional on v  2 (which includes all recovery steps), the expected valuation is E[v |
       v  2] = k2 k/2k-1 = 3 exactly.

Sketch. Part (i): v2(3n + 1) = 1 iff n  3 (mod 4), which holds iff bit 1 of n is 1. Consecutive
v = 1 steps consume trailing 1-bits one at a time (verified for 10,000 random odd integers).
Part (ii): The carry chain produces nL = 2 · 3Lm - 1, which is odd and  1 (mod 4) (since nL
is odd and its bit 1 is 0), hence v2(3nL + 1)  2. Part (iii): For uniformly random odd n with
v  2, Pr(v = k) = 1/2k-1 for k  2, giving the exact geometric sum.

Corollary C.33 (Renewal drift of generic Collatz dynamics). Decomposing the orbit into al-
ternating streak episodes (consecutive v = 1 steps) and recovery steps (v  2), the expected
log-gain per cycle (one streak + one recovery) is

            E[cycle gain] = 0.585 · E[trailing_ones - 1] + 1.585 - E[vrec] = -0.830 bits.

The expected cycle length is 2 steps, giving a per-step drift of -0.415 bits/step. This matches the
classical log2 3-2 heuristic exactly, but the decomposition into streak/recovery episodes provides
structural content: the variance per cycle is Var = 2.69, and episodes with trailing_ones  4
(probability 1/8) are net-positive at +0.116 bits/cycle, while episodes with trailing_ones  3
(probability 7/8) are strongly net-negative at -0.946 bits/cycle.

Remark C.34 (The Syracuse cocycle and modular spectral gap). A natural approach to prov-
ing universal descent is via the spectral gap of the Syracuse transfer operator. Let M  4 and
consider the 2M-1 × 2M-1 transfer matrix LM on odd residues modulo 2M , where LM (i, j) = 1
if the accelerated Collatz map sends residue i to residue j modulo 2M . The Perron eigen-
value is 1 = 4/3 (corresponding to the Haar-like invariant measure), and the spectral gap
gap(M ) := 1 - |2|/1 governs the mixing rate.

    Computational analysis for M = 4, 6, 8, 10, 12 reveals:
   · For M  8: |2| = 0, so gap = 1 (trivial mixing).
   · For M = 10: |2|  0.894, |2/1| = 0.670, gap = 0.330.
   · For M = 12: |2|  0.813, |2/1| = 0.610, gap = 0.390.
If gap(M ) stays bounded away from 0 as M  , then orbits equidistribute mod 2M for
every orbit, forcing the time-average v  2 > log2 3, which proves universal descent. This is
essentially the programme of Kontorovich­Miller [9], who showed the spectral gap exists for
small M ; extending to all M is equivalent to Collatz.
    A fundamental obstruction, however, is revealed by cycle analysis of the stochastic transition
matrix: for every M  {4, 5, . . . , 14}, there exist modular cycles with average per-step gain
exactly + log2 3  0.585 bits (residue classes where every step has v2 = 1). The maximum cycle
average gain does not decrease with M . This means no finite modular resolution can rule out
non-descending behavior: the obstruction to descent is genuinely infinite-depth, confirming the
analysis of Section F.2 from a spectral-theoretic direction.

Remark C.35 (Ruled-out approaches to universal descent). Eleven distinct approaches to
closing the gap between distributional and pointwise descent have been systematically explored
and found insufficient:

                                                            170
   1. Hybrid sieve (density-based): insufficient density decay.
   2. Pure density approach: cannot reach pointwise.
   3. Minimum descent rate: tends to 0.
   4. Azuma-type concentration: rate insufficient.
   5. Normal-number-like assumptions: circular.
   6. Transfer matrix mod 2M : positive-gain modular cycles exist for all M  10.
   7. Finite modular analysis: always insufficient at any fixed M .
   8. Paired/blocked step analysis: worst-case gain is positive (+0.585L).
   9. k  k + 4 induction on BF depth: no orbit conjugacy (multiplicative, not conjugate

       structure).
  10. Finite sieve on cumulative gain: holdout tree does not terminate.
  11. Post-cascade height loss ( approach):  < 0 for all observed BF re-entries; orbits grow

       before re-entry, so the contraction k  (1 +  - (1 + )/6) k + O(1) with  < 1 cannot
       hold.
The surviving approaches are: (a) carry propagation depth bounds (proving 3n + 1 carries
prevent O(log n)-length v = 1 streaks); (b) ergodic theory (Collatz ergodicity with respect
to a suitable measure); (c) strengthening Tao's "almost all" to "all" for structured arithmetic
progressions; (d) Walsh spectral methods for bit-mixing rate of the Collatz iteration.

Remark C.36 (Post-cascade universalization: generic  structured). A large-scale computa-
tional census (50 000 generic orbits, 18 000 post-cascade orbits from the AP family Pk + m · 81k ·
256) reveals that the BF encounter statistics are indistinguishable between the two populations:

Statistic             Post-cascade  Generic

Pr[max depth = 0]          99.0%     99.4%
Pr[max depth = 1]           1.0%     0.56%
Pr[max depth = 2]          0.03%     0.02%
Pr[k = 1 | re-entry]       97.8%     97.4%
Pr[k = 2 | re-entry]        2.2%      2.6%

This shows that the AP structure of the post-cascade family is irrelevant: the BF re-entry
behavior is governed entirely by generic Collatz dynamics. In particular, the  approach (mea-
suring height loss per unit BF depth) fails not because of the AP structure, but because generic
orbits grow before their next BF encounter.

    The BF encounter rate per step is  8.8 × 10-5 for B1 (predicted 2-14 = 6.1 × 10-5, ratio
1.44), consistent with equidistribution up to a bounded multiplicative constant. The conditional
probability of a second B1 hit within 50 steps of a first is 0.54× the unconditional rate, indicating
slight anti-correlation--favorable for independence-based arguments.

    The depth tail satisfies Pr[k  j | re-entry]  2-6(j-1), matching the prediction from
equidistribution modulo 26k+8. Combined with the orbit length bound L(n) = O(log n) (em-
pirical ratio L(n)/ log2 n  [3.1, 5.2]), the expected number of BF encounters at depth  j in
any single orbit is  C · log n · 2-(6j+8), which is < 1 when j > (log2 log n - 8)/6. This gives the
heuristic bound K(n)  O(log log n).

Remark C.37 (Carry chain scrambling: quantitative bit sensitivity). The carry chain theorem
(Theorem C.32) establishes that v  2 recovery occurs after every v = 1 streak. At each v  2
step, the map n  (3n + 1)/2v propagates carries through 3n + 1 that mix the bottom bits of
n. Computational measurement of the bit sensitivity--the expected number of output bits (of
the bottom M ) that change when a single input bit is flipped--shows:

                      171
v avg bits changed (of M = 20) fraction

2           8.5       42.5%

3           8.0       40.0%

4           7.5       37.5%

5           7.0       35.0%

Each v  2 step scrambles  40% of the bottom M bits. Since v  2 steps have density
 1/2 among all steps (Corollary C.33), the orbit undergoes bit scrambling every O(1) steps on
average.

    To maintain membership in Bk (requiring 6k + 8 specific bit values), the orbit must avoid
having the carry chain disrupt any of these bits at every v  2 step. With  40% of bits
scrambled per such step and v  2 steps occurring every  2 steps on average, the probability
of maintaining Bk membership for t steps is heuristically  (0.6)t·(6k+8)/M .

    A rigorous version of this heuristic--bounding the entropy of the conditional distribution of
T s(n) mod 26k+8 given T s-1(n)  Bk--would establish K(n) <  for all n and is the primary
formalization target for v7.

Proposition C.38 (Zero persistence of BF congruences). For every k  1 and every n  Bk,
the first Collatz iterate T (n) = (3n + 1)/2v2(3n+1) satisfies T (n) / Bk. That is, BF congruences

are destroyed in a single step.

Proof. We first establish that nk  15 (mod 32) for all k  1. The base cases are direct: n1 =
6863, n2 = 316111, n3 = 22205135, n4 = 1926419151, all satisfying nk  15 (mod 32). The
recurrence nk+4 = 224nk + 64148175 preserves this: since 224  0 (mod 32) and 64148175  15
(mod 32), we have nk+4  15 (mod 32) for all k.

    Consequently nk + 1  16 (mod 32), giving v2(nk + 1) = 4 for all k  1.
    Now for any n  nk (mod 26k+8), the first step has v = v2(3nk + 1). Since nk is odd with
nk  15 (mod 16), we have 3nk + 1  46  2 (mod 4), so v = 1. The iterate T (n) = (3n + 1)/2
is determined modulo 26k+7:

   T (n)    3nk +  1  (mod 26k+7).
                2

For T (n)  Bk we would need T (n)  nk (mod 26k+8), which requires (3nk + 1)/2  nk
(mod 26k+7), equivalently nk + 1  0 (mod 26k+8). Since v2(nk + 1) = 4 < 6k + 8 for all k  1,
this fails.

Theorem C.39 (Entropy injection for the Collatz chain mod 2M ). Let T denote the odd-to-
odd Collatz map. For each M  1, the induced map on odd residues mod2M defines a Markov

chain PM with the following properties.
   1. Transition structure. From residue r, the chain moves to 2v equally likely targets spaced
       2M-v apart, where v = v2(3r + 1 mod 2M ).
   2. Per-step entropy. The conditional Shannon entropy of T (n) mod 2M given n  r
       (mod 2M ) is exactly v bits. In particular H  1 always (since v  1 for odd r).
   3. Exact uniform stationarity. The uniform distribution (r) = 2-(M-1) over odd

       residues is the unique stationary distribution of PM .
   4. Perfect mixing in M steps. Starting from any single residue, the distribution reaches

       the uniform distribution after t = M - 1 steps in the worst case (all vi = 1). In the
       BF-specific case starting from n1 mod 214, perfect mixing occurs at step t = 10.

Proof sketch. (i) For odd r with v = v2(3r + 1 mod 2M ) and v < M , the iterate (3r + 1)/2v
is determined mod2M-v by r mod 2M . The remaining v bits (positions M -v through M -1)
depend on bits of n above position M , which are independent of n mod 2M . Since gcd(3, 2v) = 1,
these v bits are uniform.

                 172
    (ii) Follows immediately: 2v equally likely outcomes  entropy = v bits.
    (iii) Each odd residue s has exactly one preimage in each v-class: the set {r : (3r + 1)/2v  s
(mod 2M-v)} has 2v-1 odd elements mod2M , and each contributes weight 2-v, giving column

sum 1 for PM .
    (iv) After t steps with valuations v1, . . . , vt, the distribution occupies 2min(vi, M-1) equally

likely states. When vi  M - 1 the distribution is uniform. Since vi  1, this occurs after at
most M - 1 steps.

Remark C.40 (Multi-scale entropy bootstrap). The entropy injection of Theorem C.39 is
proved for the Markov model, in which bits above position M are treated as independent uniform
randomness. For the deterministic orbit of a specific integer n, these bits are fixed. However,
the same entropy injection theorem applies at resolution M  > M : the bits at positions M
through M  - 1 undergo their own Collatz-induced mixing, with entropy injection  1 bit per
step. This creates a multi-scale cascade: the "randomness" feeding level M is itself mixed at
level M , which is mixed at level M , and so on. The cascade terminates at the top bits of n
(position log2 n), which are deterministic but have only O(1) bits of entropy.

    The number of bootstrap levels is log2 n/M = O(log n/k) for Bk analysis. If each level
contributes M bits of effective entropy to the level below, the total entropy supply is O(log n)
bits--matching the full state-space dimension.

    Formalizing this multi-scale bootstrap into a rigorous proof that the Markov model accu-
rately describes the deterministic orbit remains the central open problem. Direct measurement
of the mutual information I(T t(n) mod 2M ; n mod 2M ) for deterministic orbits shows that it
drops rapidly (from M -2 bits at t = 1 to < 1 bit by t  M/2) but plateaus at a small positive
value ( 0.7 bits at M = 8,  3.8 bits at M = 10), indicating that the Markov model slightly
overestimates the mixing rate. The plateau at M = 10 is largely a finite-sample artifact (bias
 22(M-1)/2Nsamples); the genuine residual correlation at M = 8 is  0.3 bits.

    The Ising analogy provides a physical framework: the Collatz orbit is a one-dimensional
chain with positive coupling (bit correlations) and positive temperature (carry-chain entropy
injection). In one dimension, the Ising model has no phase transition: any positive temperature
destroys long-range order. The spectral data from the Collatz transition matrix gives Ising
correlation length  < 0.3 steps for M  12.

    A subtlety: the second eigenvalue |2(PM )| increases with M (from 0.0003 at M = 6 to
0.022 at M = 12), so the spectral gap shrinks. Nonetheless, the per-step entropy injection of
 1 bit guarantees perfect mixing (in the Markov model) in at most M - 1 steps regardless of
the spectral gap, because the distribution support doubles at each v = 1 step and multiplies
by 2v at each v  2 step. The spectral gap controls the rate of exponential convergence after
mixing, not the mixing time itself.

    The v = 1 component of PM acts as an iterated function system (IFS) on Z/2M-1Z: two
branches f0(x) = 3x + 2 and f1(x) = 3x + 2 + 2M-2, each with probability 1/2. The Lyapunov
exponent log2 3 > 0 makes this an expanding IFS, ensuring spectral gap > 0 for each fixed M .
Whether the gap is uniformly bounded below as M   is the quantitative form of the
remaining open problem.

Fourier mode analysis of the IFS. The v = 1 IFS noise has a precise Fourier structure:

the two branches differ by translation 2M-2, so the noise measure's Fourier transform is µ^() =

1  (1  +  ei )  =  1 even.  This means:  (i) all odd Fourier modes  are annihilated in a single
2

v = 1 step (µ^() = 0); (ii) all even modes are untouched (µ^() = 1). Since multiplication by

3 preserves the 2-adic valuation of  (that is, v2(3) = v2()), the even modes form a closed

invariant subspace under the v = 1 operator.

       Mixing of the even modes requires v  2 steps. A v = k step consumes k bits from the

2-adic expansion, mapping modes with v2() = j to modes with v2() = j - k (effectively).

Since v  2 occurs with probability 1/2 among odd residues, each step has probability 1/2 of

                                              173
producing a mode-mixing event. After t steps with  events of v  2, at most 2-(1+) fraction
of Fourier modes survive.

    Since E[ | t] = t/2, after M - 1 steps:

                      survival fraction  2-(1+(M-1)/2) = 2-(M+1)/2.

This gives a per-step spectral bound 2(PM )  (1 - 2-(M+1)/2)1/(M-1)  1 - 1/M , explaining
quantitatively why the gap shrinks as O(1/M ) while the mixing time remains O(M ). This is
verified numerically: the predicted per-step 2 bounds (0.98 at M = 6, 0.994 at M = 8, 0.998
at M = 10) are consistent with the computed values.

Remark C.42 (Skew-product formulation and the Galperin analogy). The distributional-to-
pointwise gap can be formulated as a skew-product on (, ), where   {0, 1}N is the 2-adic
symbolic coordinate (the Bernstein­Lagarias conjugacy makes Collatz act as a shift on ) and
  R/Z is an Archimedean phase variable recording cumulative growth discrepancies:

                                       t+1 = t + (log2 3 - vi) (mod 1),

where vi = v2(3ni + 1) is the valuation at step i. The quantity t = ti=1(log2 3 - vi) measures
the cumulative deviation of log2 nt from its expected drift.

    Under the Markov model, E[vi] = 2, giving E[log2 3 - vi] = log2 3 - 2  -0.415, so t  -
(descent). The pointwise question is: can t be trapped in a bounded region for a specific n?

    This formulation echoes the Galperin billiards paradigm (where counting collisions between
a heavy and light ball yields digits of  via a change to angular coordinates). The transfer-
able insight is not "replace 3 by " but rather: look for a coordinate system in which the
piecewise-linear Collatz dynamics becomes a winding/rotation process, where long-term behav-
ior is controlled by an irrational rotation number (here, log2 3).

    The native irrational constant for Collatz is log2 3, not : the arithmetic tension is between
multiplication by 3 and division by powers of 2, making log 3/ log 2 the natural phase parameter.

                                                 174
A deterministic discrepancy theorem--proving that no integer can keep t aligned with low-

valuation sectors (vi = 1) indefinitely--would close the distributional-to-pointwise gap. This

is equivalent to showing that the symbolic-times-phase skew-product (, Rlog2 3) on {0, 1}N × T
has no invariant curves supporting integer orbits.

Remark C.43 (v = 1 streak structure and the adversary budget). The -variable can only
grow during blocks with v¯ < log2 3  1.585, which requires v = 1 at most steps. We prove three
structural results:

(i) v = 1 streak density. Among odd residues modulo 2s+2, exactly 2 out of 2s+1 allow a
v = 1 streak of length s. These are n  2s+1 - 1 (mod 2s+2) and n  2s - 1 (mod 2s+1). The
density 2-s matches the independence prediction exactly, for all s tested (s  15).

(ii) No sustained low-v cycles. An exhaustive search over all periodic v-patterns of period
1 through 8 with average < log2 3 finds zero realizable as modular cycles of the Collatz map.
The BF pattern [1, 1, 1, 3] (average 1.5) is realizable as a finite cascade but self-terminates (zero
persistence).

(iii) The adversary's -budget. Under the Markov model, the expected -change from a BF
encounter at depth k (including the wait time to reach Bk) is

               E[k] = k · log2(81/64) - (log2 3 - 2) · 26k+7 < 0 for all k  1.

                         +0.34k     +0.415·26k+7

The BF gain is O(k) but the expected wait is (26k), making each encounter a net loss for the
adversary. Thus t is a supermartingale: t  - almost surely under the Markov model.

Empirical confirmation. The vi autocorrelation along deterministic orbits is +0.040 at lag 1
and < 0.045 for all lags  20. The conditional probability Pr(vi+1 = 1 | vi = 1) = 1.030×Pr(vi =

Remark C.47 (Route A obstruction and Route B pullback program). Route A obstruction

(identified by GPT): the cycle envelope coefficient 1 +  = 1 + log2(81/64)/6 = 1.0566 . . . > 1,

  (1 + )k + O(1): restart depths do not contract. This kills any naive Borel­Cantelli

argument from size alone.

    Post-cascade convergence. All Pk for k = 1, . . . , 5000 (P5000 has 31,700 bits) reach 1 with zero
BF re-entries. Orbit lengths grow as  2.3 log2 Pk, consistent with generic Collatz dynamics.
The low-bit structure is Pk  111 (mod 128) for all k (7-bit deterministic prefix), extending
the earlier 47 (mod 64). The 10-step prefix from 47 (mod 64) has ratio 310/214 = 3.604 > 1
(expansive), but the stopping time (Pk) is bounded:   [8, 64] for k  500, mean  16.6,
